and the equality holds if and only if
I^{d}\cap I_{0}
is an interval,
I^{d,+}=\emptyset
and
\Lambda_{d}=\{(2n_{d}-2\#I^{d}\cap I_{0}-1,\iota)\}
for some
\iota\in S
. (Note that
I^{d,+}\neq\emptyset
would force
k_{d}\geq 2n_{d}-1>2\#I^{d,-}+1
.)

•

If
I^{d}\cap I_{0}\neq\emptyset
and there exists
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
such that
d=r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1
, then either
I^{d,+}=I^{d,-}=\emptyset
(with
k_{d}\geq 0
) or
I^{d,+}=\emptyset\neq I^{d,-}
(with
k_{d}\geq 2n_{d}-2n_{d}^{-}+1
) or
I^{d,+}\neq\emptyset
(with
k_{d}\geq 2n_{d}-1
). So we always have
k_{d}\geq 2n_{d}-2\#I^{d}\cap I_{0}-2
and the equality holds if and only if
I^{d,+}=I^{d,-}=\emptyset
.

Consequently, for each
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
, we have

\sum_{d=r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1}^{r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}}k_{d}\geq-1+\sum_{d=r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1}^{r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}}(2n_{d}-2\#I^{d}\cap I_{0}-1).

Summing over
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
, we have

\sum_{d=1}^{r_{I}}k_{d}\geq-r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}+2n-r_{I}-2\#I_{0},

which together with
k_{0}\geq r_{I_{v}}-1
implies that

k=\sum_{d=0}^{r_{I}}k_{d}\geq r_{I_{v}}-1-r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}+2n-r_{I}-2\#I_{0}=(n-\#I_{v})-1-(n-\#I_{v}\cap I_{1})+2n-(n-\ell)-2\#I_{0}\\
=\ell+n-1+\#I_{v}\cap I_{1}-\#I_{v}-2\#I_{0}=\ell+n-1+\#I_{1}-\#I_{v}\cup I_{1}-2\#I_{0}\geq\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}.

The precise conditions on
\Theta
for the equality
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}
to hold are clear from the discussion above. The case
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1
is similar and follows from the following observation: if
\Theta
is a maximally
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuple such that
\max\{m\mid(m,\iota)\in\Lambda_{d}\}>2\#I^{d,-}+1
for some
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
and
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}
satisfying
I^{d}\cap I_{0}\neq\emptyset
, then we must have
k\geq\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+2
.
∎

2.4.
A combinatorial subcomplex of
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,k}

In this section, we construct a combinatorial subcomplex of
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,k}
for each
k\geq 0
using
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples. The main result here is Proposition
2.23
, where we prove that this combinatorial subcomplex is quasi-isomorphic to
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,k}
.

Definition 2.17
.

We consider all sets of the form
\mathbf{I}_{>d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}(I^{d^{\prime\prime}})_{d^{\prime\prime}>d}
for some
I_{0}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{v}\cap I_{1}
with
\#I=\ell
and
1\leq d\leq r_{I}=n-\ell
. Then the set
\{\mathbf{I}_{>d}\}_{I,d}
admits a natural partial order described as follows. Given
\mathbf{I}_{>d}
and
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{>d^{\prime}}
for some
(I,d)
and
(I^{\prime},d^{\prime})
, we say that
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{>d^{\prime}}<\mathbf{I}_{>d}
if exactly one of the following holds

•

d^{\prime}<d
and
(I^{\prime})^{d^{\prime\prime}}=I^{d^{\prime\prime}}
for each
d^{\prime\prime}>d
;

•

there exists
d_{\flat}\geq\max\{d,d^{\prime}\}
such that
(I^{\prime})^{d_{\flat}}\subsetneq I^{d_{\flat}}
and
(I^{\prime})^{d^{\prime\prime}}=I^{d^{\prime\prime}}
for each
d^{\prime\prime}>d_{\flat}
.

It is not difficult to check that this is a well defined partial order on
\{\mathbf{I}_{>d}\}_{I,d}
.

For each
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard
\Theta
, we set
i_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d=1}^{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}-1}n_{d}\in(I_{v}\cap I_{1})\setminus I
and consider
\Theta^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}p_{i_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}}^{+}(\Theta)
which is clearly well defined. For each
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard
\Theta
, we associate the set
\mathbf{I}_{\Theta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}(I^{d})_{d>d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}}
.

Lemma 2.18
.

For each
v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}
with
\#v=k_{0}
, the subset

\{d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{\Theta^{+}})\mid\Theta=(v,I,\underline{k},\underline{\Lambda})\text{ is }(I_{0},I_{1})\text{-standard },|\underline{k}|=k,\#I=\ell\}\subseteq E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell,k}

is linearly independent.

Proof.

Let
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})
be a
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard tuple satisfying
\Theta^{\prime}\neq\Theta
and

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}\left(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{\Theta^{+}})\right)\neq 0.

Then we have the following possibilities

•

If
d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}<d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}-1
, then we have
\Theta^{\prime}<_{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}}p_{i_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}^{\prime}}^{+}(p_{i_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime}))
with
i_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d=1}^{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}}n_{d}\in(I_{v}\cap I_{1})\setminus I
, which contradicts the fact that
\Theta^{\prime}
is
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard.

•

If
d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}=d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}-1
, then we have
\mathbf{I}_{\Theta^{\prime}}=\mathbf{I}_{\Theta}\sqcup\{(I^{\prime})^{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}}\}
.

•

If
d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}\geq d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}
, then
I^{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}+1}\cap I_{0}\neq\emptyset
, and
\Theta^{+}=p_{i}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
with
i\in I^{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}+1,-}
. Moreover, the tuple
\mathbf{I}_{\Theta^{\prime}}=\{(I^{\prime})^{d}\mid d>d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}\}
satisfies
(I^{\prime})^{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}+1}\subsetneq I^{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}+1}
and
(I^{\prime})^{d}=I^{d}
for each
d>d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta^{\prime}}+1
.

Hence, we always have
\mathbf{I}_{\Theta^{\prime}}<\mathbf{I}_{\Theta}
for partial order introduced in Definition
2.17
. The proof is thus finished by induction on this partial order.
∎

Lemma 2.19
.

For each
v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}
and
0\neq x\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell,k}
, there exists
x^{\prime}\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell-1,k}
such that

c_{\Theta}(x-d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x^{\prime}))=0

for each
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard
\Theta=(v,I,\underline{k},\underline{\Lambda})
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemma
2.18
. In fact, we can construct
x^{\prime}
as a linear combination of various
x_{\Theta^{+}}
for
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard tuples
\Theta
, by an induction on the partial order introduced in Definition
2.17
.
∎

For each tuple
\Theta
, we define an integer

e_{\Theta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d=1}^{r_{I}}\max\{m\mid(m,\iota)\in\Lambda_{d}\}

which will be useful in our later induction argument. (Here we use the convention
\max\{m\mid(m,\iota)\in\emptyset\}=0
.) It has the following simple property.

Lemma 2.20
.

Let
\Theta
be a tuple.

•

If
i\in(I_{v}\cap I_{1})\setminus I
and
p_{i}^{+}(\Theta)
is defined, then
e_{p_{i}^{+}(\Theta)}\leq e_{\Theta}
. Moreover,
e_{p_{i}^{+}(\Theta)}=e_{\Theta}
if and only if either
\Lambda_{d}=\emptyset
or
\Lambda_{d+1}=\emptyset
, where
1\leq d\leq r_{I}
satisfies
\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d}n_{d^{\prime}}=i
.

•

If
i\in I
and
p_{i}^{-}(\Theta)
is defined, then
e_{p_{i}^{-}(\Theta)}=e_{\Theta}
.

Proof.

This is immediate from the definition of
p_{i}^{+}(\Theta)
(resp.
p_{i}^{-}(\Theta)
). We use the fact that, if
\Lambda_{d}\cap\Lambda_{d+1}=\emptyset
, then

\max\{m\mid(m,\iota)\in\Lambda_{d}\sqcup\Lambda_{d+1}\}\leq\max\{m\mid(m,\iota)\in\Lambda_{d}\}+\max\{m\mid(m,\iota)\in\Lambda_{d+1}\},

and the equality holds if and only if either
\Lambda_{d}=\emptyset
or
\Lambda_{d+1}=\emptyset
.
∎

For each bidegree
(-\ell,k)
, we define
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond}^{-\ell,k}\subseteq E_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
as the subspace spanned by
x_{\Omega}
where
\Omega
runs through equivalence classes of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples satisfying
(-\ell_{\Omega},k_{\Omega})=(-\ell,k)
. As different equivalence classes of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples do not intersect, it is clear that
\{x_{\Omega}\mid(-\ell_{\Omega},k_{\Omega})=(-\ell,k)\}
is a basis of
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond}^{-\ell,k}
, which induces a basis of
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell,k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond}^{-\ell,k}\cap E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell,k}
for each
v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}
.

Lemma 2.21
.

We have
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell,k})\subseteq E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell+1,k}
for each
1\leq\ell\leq n-1
,
k\geq 0
and
v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}
. In particular,
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{\bullet,k}
is a subcomplex of
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{\bullet,k}
for each
k\geq 0
and
v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell+1,k}
for each equivalence class
\Omega
of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples satisfying
(-\ell_{\Omega},k_{\Omega})=(-\ell,k)
. Let
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})
be a tuple satisfying

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Res}_{n,I,I\setminus\{i\}}^{k}(x_{\Theta}))\neq 0

for some
\Theta=(v,I,\underline{k},\underline{\Lambda})\in\Omega
and
i\in I
. Then we only have the following two possibilities

•

The tuple
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})
is not
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic, and there exists a unique
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
and
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+2\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}-1
such that
k^{\prime}_{d}=0
(namely
\Lambda^{\prime}_{d}=\emptyset
). Moreover, if we write
i_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}
and
i_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}
, then
\Theta\in\{p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}),p_{i_{2}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\}\subseteq\Omega
, and moreover any
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega
that satisfies
c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Res}_{n,I^{\prime\prime},I^{\prime\prime}\setminus\{i^{\prime\prime}\}}^{k}(x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}))\neq 0
(with
\Theta^{\prime\prime}=(v,I^{\prime\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime})\in\Omega
and
i^{\prime\prime}\in I^{\prime\prime}
) must be either
p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
or
p_{i_{2}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
. Note that we have

\varepsilon(p_{i_{2}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))=(-1)^{i_{2}-i_{1}}\varepsilon(p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))

and

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{p_{i_{2}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))=(-1)^{m(I^{\prime}\sqcup\{i_{2}\},i_{2})-m(I^{\prime}\sqcup\{i_{1}\},i_{1})}c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))=(-1)^{i_{2}-i_{1}-1}c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})})).

Consequently, we have

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega}))=\varepsilon(p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))+\varepsilon(p_{i_{2}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{p_{i_{2}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))\\
=\varepsilon(p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))(1+(-1)^{i_{2}-i_{1}}(-1)^{i_{2}-i_{1}-1})=0.

•

The tuple
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})
is
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic and we write
\Omega^{\prime}
for its equivalence class. For each
\Theta^{\prime\prime}=(v,I^{\prime\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime})\in\Omega^{\prime}
, there exists a unique
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}\in(I_{v}\cap I_{1})\setminus I^{\prime\prime}
such that
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime})=(v,I^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime})\in\Omega
(by comparing
\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\underline{\Lambda}
and
\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime}
). This gives a natural map

\Omega^{\prime}\rightarrow\Omega:~\Theta^{\prime\prime}\mapsto p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime}).

Using the basis of
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell+1,k}
of the form
\{x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}\}
for various tuples
\Theta^{\prime\prime}
, we define
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\Omega^{\prime}}^{-\ell,k}
as the composition of
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
with the projection to the subspace spanned by
\{x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}\mid\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}\}
. We claim that there exists
\varepsilon(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})\in\{1,-1\}
such that
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\Omega^{\prime}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})=\varepsilon(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})x_{\Omega^{\prime}}
. In fact, if we pick up a
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
(which determines a
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime})\in\Omega
) as above, it suffices to show that
\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime})\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})c_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}))

is independent of the choice of
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
. It is clear that
\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime})\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})=(-1)^{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}}
and

c_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}))=(-1)^{m(I^{\prime\prime\prime},i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}})}c_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}(\mathrm{Res}_{n,I^{\prime\prime\prime},I^{\prime\prime}}^{k}(x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}})).

Then we observe that both
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}-m(I^{\prime\prime\prime},i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}})
and
c_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}(\mathrm{Res}_{n,I^{\prime\prime\prime},I^{\prime\prime}\}}^{k}(x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}))
depend only on
\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime}
and
\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime}
, so does
\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime})\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})c_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}))
. As all different choices of
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
share the same
\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime}
and
\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
\varepsilon(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime})\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})c_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}))
is well defined.

As a summary, we deduce that

(2.11)

d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})=\sum_{\Omega^{\prime}}\varepsilon(\Omega,\Omega^{\prime})x_{\Omega^{\prime}}\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell+1,k}

where
\Omega^{\prime}
runs through all equivalence classes of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples that appear in the second possibility above. The proof is thus finished.
∎

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}
and
x\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell,k}
such that
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x)\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell+1,k}
and
c_{\Theta}(x)=0
for each
\Theta=(v,I,\underline{k},\underline{\Lambda})
which is either
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard or maximally
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic. Then
x=0
.

Proof.

For each tuple
\Theta=(v,I,\underline{k},\underline{\Lambda})
, we define
d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}
as the maximal integer satisfying
k_{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}}=0
and
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+2\leq d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}
for some
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
if it exists, and as
1
otherwise. This clearly extends the definition of
d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}
when
\Theta
is
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard. We assume inductively that
c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(x)=0
for all
\Theta^{\prime}
satisfying either
\Theta<_{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}}\Theta^{\prime}
or
e_{\Theta^{\prime}}<e_{\Theta}.

It is harmless to assume that
\Theta
is neither
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard nor maximally
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic. Hence, there exists
s\leq s_{1}\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
and
\max\{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta},r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{1}-1}+1\}\leq d_{1}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{1}}
and
i_{1}\in I^{d_{1}}
such that
p_{i_{1}}^{-}(\Theta)
is defined. We choose
d_{1}
and
i_{1}
to be maximal possible, and thus
p_{i_{1}}^{-}(\Theta)
is
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard. If
c_{\Theta}(x)\neq 0
, then there exists
i_{2}\in(I_{v}\cap I_{1})\setminus I
such that
c_{p_{i_{2}}^{+}p_{i_{1}}^{-}(\Theta)}(x)\neq 0
. If
i_{2}<i_{1}
, then
p_{i_{2}}^{+}p_{i_{1}}^{-}(\Theta)
is
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard and thus contradicts our assumption. If
i_{2}>\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{1}}n_{d^{\prime}}
, then as
\Lambda_{d^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
for each
d^{\prime}>d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}
, we deduce from Lemma
2.20
that
e_{p_{i_{2}}^{+}p_{i_{1}}^{-}(\Theta)}<e_{\Theta}
, which contradicts our inductive assumption. Hence, we must have
i_{2}=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{1}}n_{d^{\prime}}
and thus
\Theta<_{d_{I_{0},I_{1},\Theta}}p_{i_{2}}^{+}p_{i_{1}}^{-}(\Theta)
, another contradiction. The proof is thus finished.
∎

Proposition 2.23
.

For each
v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}
and
k\geq 0
, the subcomplex

E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{\bullet,k}\rightarrow E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{\bullet,k}

induces an quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We fix an
\#I_{0}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{1}
and
k\geq 0
.

We first show that the induced map on cohomology is surjective, namely for each
x\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell,k}
satisfying
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell,k}(x)=0
, there exists
x^{\prime}\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell-1,k}
and
x^{\prime\prime}\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell,k}
such that
x=x^{\prime\prime}+d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x^{\prime})
. In fact, given such
x
, we can choose
x^{\prime}
as in Lemma
2.19
, and then take

x^{\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{\Omega}c_{\Theta}(x-d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x^{\prime}))\varepsilon(\Theta)x_{\Omega}\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell,k}

where
\Omega
runs through all equivalence classes of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples with the fixed bidegree
(-\ell,k)
and
v
as above, and
\Theta\in\Omega
is maximally
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic with sign
\varepsilon(\Theta)\in\{1,-1\}
as in Definition
2.13
. The equality
x=x^{\prime\prime}+d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x^{\prime})
then follows from Lemma
2.22
.

Now we show that the induced map on cohomology is injective, namely for each
y\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell,k}
, and
y^{\prime}\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{-\ell-1,k}
satisfying
y=d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(y^{\prime})
, there exists
y^{\prime\prime}\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell-1,k}
such that
y=d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(y^{\prime\prime})
. By Lemma
2.19
we may assume that
c_{\Theta}(y^{\prime})=0
for each
(I_{0},I_{1})
-standard
\Theta
. Then we take

y^{\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{\Omega}c_{\Theta}(y^{\prime})\varepsilon(\Theta)x_{\Omega}\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell-1,k}

where
\Omega
runs through all equivalence classes of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples with the fixed bidegree
(-\ell-1,k)
and
v
as above and
\Theta\in\Omega
is maximally
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic with sign
\varepsilon(\Theta)\in\{1,-1\}
as in Definition
2.13
. Then we have

d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime})=y-d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(y^{\prime\prime})\in E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond,v}^{-\ell,k},

which together with Lemma
2.22
(with bidegree
(-\ell,k)
there replaced with
(-\ell-1,k)
) implies that
y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime}=0
and thus
y=d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(y^{\prime\prime})
. The proof is thus finished.
∎

Remark 2.24
.

If
I_{0}=I_{0}^{1}
(namely
I_{0}=\{1,\dots,\#I_{0}\}
), then each
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuple is automatically maximal and thus each equivalence class of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples contains exactly one element. Moreover, one can check that
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond}^{-\ell,k})=0
and thus
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond}^{-\ell,k}\cong E_{2,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
for each bidegree
(-\ell,k)
in this case.

2.5.
Computations in bottom two degrees

In this section, we finish the computation of
\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h}(F_{n,\Delta_{n}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda))
when
h\in\{\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0},\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1\}
.

Lemma 2.25
.

Let
\Omega
be an equivalence class of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples with
(-\ell_{\Omega},k_{\Omega})=(-\ell,k)
.

(i)

If
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}
, then
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})=0
.

(ii)

If
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1
, then
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})\neq 0
if and only if
I_{v}\cup I_{1}=\Delta_{n}
and there exists a unique
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
satisfying
\Lambda_{r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1}\neq\emptyset
and
I^{d}\cap I_{0}\neq\emptyset
for some
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}
.
Moreover, if
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})\neq 0
, then there exists a unique equivalence class
\Omega^{\prime}
of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples such that
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})=\pm x_{\Omega^{\prime}}
, and
\Omega^{\prime}
also determines
\Omega
uniquely.

Proof.

Let
\Omega^{\prime}
be an arbitrary equivalence class of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples that appears in (
2.11
), then it is clear that
k_{\Omega^{\prime}}=k_{\Omega}
and
\ell_{\Omega^{\prime}}=\ell_{\Omega}-1
. By the choice of
\Omega^{\prime}
(see the second possibility of Lemma
2.21
), there exists
\Theta=(v,I,\underline{k},\underline{\Lambda})\in\Omega
,
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})\in\Omega^{\prime}
and
i\in I\setminus I^{\prime}
such that
\Theta=p_{i}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
. We note from Lemma
2.16
that
\#\Lambda_{d}\leq 1
for each
1\leq d\leq r_{I}
. If
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}
, then there must exist
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
and
r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s-1}+2\leq d^{\prime}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s}
such that
\Lambda^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=\emptyset
, which contradicts the fact that
\Theta^{\prime}
is
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic. Item
(ii)
thus follows. Similar argument works for the case
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1
and either
\#I_{v}\cup I_{1}=n-2
or there exists a unique
i\in\Delta_{n}\setminus I_{v}
such that
\#v\cap\mathcal{B}_{n,\Delta_{n}\setminus\{i\}}\geq 2
. It suffices to treat the case
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1
,
I_{v}\cup I_{1}=\Delta_{n}
and
k_{0}=\#\Delta_{n}\setminus I_{v}
, and there exists a unique
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
such that
\Lambda_{r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1}\neq\emptyset
. If
I^{d}\cap I_{0}=\emptyset
for all
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}
, the same argument as in item
(ii)
proves that
\Omega^{\prime}
cannot exist. If
I^{d}\cap I_{0}\neq\emptyset
for some
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s-1}+1\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}
, then we have
I^{d,-}\neq\emptyset
and
\Lambda_{d}=\{(2\#I^{d,-}+1,\iota)\}
(for some
\iota\in S
) for each such
d
, and
\Omega^{\prime}
is characterized by the following conditions

•

r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}=r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s^{\prime}}
for each
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq s-1
and
r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}=r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s^{\prime}}+1
for each
s\leq s^{\prime}\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
;

•

\Lambda^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=\Lambda_{d^{\prime}}
for each
1\leq d^{\prime}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s-1}
,
\Lambda^{\prime}_{r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s-1}+1}=\emptyset
and
\Lambda^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=\Lambda_{d^{\prime}-1}
for each
r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s-1}+2\leq d^{\prime}\leq r_{I^{\prime}}
.

It is then clear that
\Omega
and
\Omega^{\prime}
uniquely determine each other, and thus item
(iii)
follows.
∎

Let
\#I_{0}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{1}
be an integer. We write
\Psi_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
for the set of equivalence classes
\Omega
of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples satisfying
(-\ell_{\Omega},k_{\Omega})=(-\ell,\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0})
if
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}
. We write
\Psi_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
for the set of equivalence classes
\Omega
of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples satisfying
(-\ell_{\Omega},k_{\Omega})=(-\ell,\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1)
and
d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})=0
(see item
(iii)
of Lemma
2.25
) if
k=\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1
.

Recall that
E_{2,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}=\mathrm{ker}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k})/\mathrm{im}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k})
for each
\#I_{0}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{1}
and
k\geq 0
.

Proposition 2.26
.

For each
\#I_{0}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{1}
, the subset

(2.12)

\{x_{\Omega}\}_{\Omega\in\Psi_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}}\subseteq\mathrm{ker}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k})

induces a basis of
E_{2,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
if
k\in\{\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0},\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1\}
.

Proof.

This follows clearly from Proposition
2.23
and Lemma
2.25
. Note that
E_{1,I_{0},I_{1},\diamond}^{-\ell,\ell+\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}-1}=0
by Lemma
2.16
.
∎

Lemma 2.27
.

The spectral sequence
\{E_{r,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}\}_{r\geq 1,\#I_{0}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{1},k\geq 0}
degenerates at the second page.

Proof.

We set
\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}^{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\{\mathrm{val}_{i}\mid i\in\Delta_{n}\}
and there exists a natural map between power sets
2^{\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}}\rightarrow 2^{\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}^{\infty}}
by mapping
v
to the intersection
v\cap\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}^{\infty}
.
It is clear that we have a decomposition

E_{\bullet,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}\cong\bigoplus_{v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}}E_{\bullet,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{\bullet,\bullet}

into its
v
-isotypic components and we set

{}_{v^{\infty}}E_{\bullet,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}\cong\bigoplus_{v\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset},v\cap\mathcal{B}_{n,\emptyset}^{\infty}=v^{\infty}}E_{\bullet,I_{0},I_{1},v}^{\bullet,\bullet}

with differential
{}_{v^{\infty}}d_{\bullet,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
.
Hence, it suffices to show that
{}_{v^{\infty}}d_{2,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}=0
. The spectral sequence
{}_{v^{\infty}}E_{\bullet,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
actually arises from the double complex
{}_{v^{\infty}}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
with

{}_{v^{\infty}}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}\cong\bigoplus_{I_{0}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{v^{\infty}}\cap I_{1},\#I=\ell}\wedge^{k}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}_{n,I}^{\ast}

with the column complexes being direct sum of Koszul complexes for various Levi, and differential of row complexes being direct sum of

(-1)^{m(I,i)}\mathrm{Res}_{n,I,I\setminus\{i\}}^{k}:\wedge^{k}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}_{n,I}^{\ast}\rightarrow\wedge^{k}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}_{n,I\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}

with
I_{0}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{v^{\infty}}\cap I_{1}
and
i\in I\setminus I_{0}
. Following Section 14 of
[
BT82
]
we write
\delta
for the row differential and
D
for the column differential of the double complex
{}_{v^{\infty}}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
. Note that
{}_{v^{\infty}}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
contains a sub double complex
{}_{v^{\infty},\natural}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
with

{}_{v^{\infty},\natural}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}\cong\bigoplus_{I_{0}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{v^{\infty}}\cap I_{1},\#I=\ell}(\wedge^{k}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}_{n,I}^{\ast})^{\overline{\mathfrak{l}}_{n,I}}.

Note that
D
restricts to zero on
{}_{v^{\infty},\natural}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
and in fact the embedding
{}_{v^{\infty},\natural}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,\bullet}\hookrightarrow_{v^{\infty}}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,\bullet}
is a quasi-isomorphism for each
\#I_{0}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{1}
. Then we observe that
x_{\Omega}\in_{v^{\infty}}E_{2,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
(for each equivalence class
\Omega
of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuple that show up) can be lifted to an element of
\widetilde{x}_{\Omega}\in~_{v^{\infty},\natural}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}\subseteq~_{v^{\infty}}E_{0,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
satisfying
D(\widetilde{x}_{\Omega})=0
and
\delta(\widetilde{x}_{\Omega})=0
, which implies that
{}_{v^{\infty}}d_{2,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}(x_{\Omega})=0
(using the description of
{}_{v^{\infty}}d_{2,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,k}
on page 162 of
[
BT82
]
). The proof is thus finished.
∎

Theorem 2.28
.

Let
I_{0}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be a pair of subsets.

•

If
\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h}(F_{n,\Delta_{n}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda))\neq 0
, then
\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}\leq h\leq n^{2}-n
.

•

For each
h\in\{\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0},\#I_{1}-2\#I_{0}+1\}
, the space
M_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h}(F_{n,\Delta_{n}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda))
admits a canonical decreasing filtration

0=\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{0}+1}(M_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{h})\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{0}}(M_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{h})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{1}}(M_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{h})=M_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{h}

such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{-\ell}(M_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{h})/\mathrm{Fil}^{-\ell+1}(M_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{h})\cong E_{2,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,\ell+h}
admits a basis indexed by
\Psi_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell,\ell+h}
for each
\#I_{0}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{1}
.

Proof.

This follows directly from Proposition
2.26
and Lemma
2.27
.
∎

2.6.
Twisted
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuples

In this section, we consider a variant of
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuple as in Definition
2.13
. Let
\Theta=(v,I,\underline{k},\underline{\Lambda})
be a maximally
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuple and
\Omega
be its equivalence class.
We consider an integer
1\leq s_{0}\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
that satisfies

Condition 2.29
.

•

I^{r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1}\subseteq I_{0}
and
\Lambda_{r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1}=\emptyset
;

•

\Lambda_{d}=\{(2n_{d}-1,\iota_{d})\}
for some
\iota_{d}\in S
, for each
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+2\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}}
satisfying
I^{d}\cap I_{0}=\emptyset
;

•

I^{d,+}=\emptyset
,
I^{d,-}\neq\emptyset
and
\Lambda_{d}=\{(2\#I^{d,-}+1,\iota_{d})\}
for some
\iota_{d}\in S
, for each
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+2\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}}
satisfying
I^{d}\cap I_{0}\neq\emptyset
.

Then we choose a
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d_{0}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}}
and write
i_{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d}n_{d^{\prime}}
and
i_{d}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\max\{i\in I\setminus I_{0}\mid i<i_{d}\}
for each
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d\leq d_{0}
. Then we set

\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}=(v,I^{s_{0},d_{0}},\underline{k}^{s_{0},d_{0}},\underline{\Lambda}^{s_{0},d_{0}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}p_{i_{d_{0}}^{\prime}}^{-}p_{i_{d_{0}-1}}^{+}\cdots p_{i_{2}^{\prime}}^{-}p_{i_{1}}^{+}(\Theta)

and call it the
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twist of
\Theta
. Note that
\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}
is well-defined thanks to Condition
2.29
and satisfies
\Lambda^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d}=\Lambda_{d+1}
for each
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d\leq d_{0}-1
,
\Lambda^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d_{0}}=\emptyset
and
\Lambda^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d}=\Lambda_{d}
for each
d_{0}+1\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}}
. In particular,
\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}
satisfies

Condition 2.30
.

•

(I^{s_{0},d_{0}})^{d_{0}}\subseteq I_{0}
and
\Lambda_{d_{0}}=\emptyset
;

•

\Lambda_{d}=\{(2n_{d}-1,\iota_{d})\}
for some
\iota_{d}\in S
, for each
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}}
satisfying
d\neq d_{0}
and
I^{d}\cap I_{0}=\emptyset
;

•

I^{d,+}=\emptyset
,
I^{d,-}\neq\emptyset
and
\Lambda_{d}=\{(2\#I^{d,-}+1,\iota_{d})\}
for some
\iota_{d}\in S
, for each
d_{0}+1\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}}
satisfying
I^{d}\cap I_{0}\neq\emptyset
.

•

I^{d,-}=\emptyset
,
I^{d,+}\neq\emptyset
and
\Lambda_{d}=\{(2\#I^{d,+}+1,\iota_{d})\}
for some
\iota_{d}\in S
, for each
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d\leq d_{0}-1
satisfying
I^{d}\cap I_{0}\neq\emptyset
.

Given two tuples
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})
and
\Theta^{\prime\prime}=(v,I^{\prime\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime\prime})
, we say that
\Theta^{\prime\prime}
is a
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted improvement
of
\Theta^{\prime}
of level
d
if one of the following holds

•

\Theta^{\prime\prime}
is an improvement of
\Theta^{\prime}
of level
d
with
d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}
or
d\geq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}+1
(see Definition
2.12
);

•

d\geq d_{0}
and there exists
i\in(I^{\prime})^{d}\setminus I_{0}
and
i^{\prime}=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d}n_{d^{\prime}}\in(I_{v}\cap I_{1})\setminus I^{\prime}
such that
i\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
and
\Theta^{\prime\prime}=p_{i^{\prime}}^{+}p_{i}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime})
;

•

d\leq d_{0}-1
and there exists
i\in(I^{\prime\prime})^{d}\setminus I_{0}
and
i^{\prime}=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d}n_{d^{\prime}}\in(I_{v}\cap I_{1})\setminus I^{\prime\prime}
such that
i^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
and
\Theta^{\prime}=p_{i^{\prime}}^{+}p_{i}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime\prime})
.

Similar to Definition
2.12
, we can use the notion
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted improvement to define
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted smaller
and
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted equivalent
. Then it is easy to see that
\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}
is
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted smaller than no other tuple, and thus is
maximally
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic
. Then a tuple is
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic
if it is
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted smaller than some tuple of the form
\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}
. Given
\Omega
above, we write
\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
for the
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted equivalence class of
\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}
, and call it the
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twist of
\Omega
. Finally, we set
x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{\Theta\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}}\varepsilon(\Theta)x_{\Theta}
.

Lemma 2.31
.

Let
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})
which has the same bidegree
(-\ell,k)
as
\Theta
. Assume that
\Theta
satisfies Condition
2.29
and
\Theta^{\prime}
satisfies

•

r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s}=r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s}
for each
1\leq s\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
;

•

(I^{\prime})^{d}=I^{d}
for each
1\leq d\leq r_{I^{\prime}}
satisfying either
d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}
or
d\geq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}+1
.

Then
\Theta^{\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
if and only if the following holds

(i)

\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime}=\underline{\Lambda}^{s_{0},d_{0}}
;

(ii)

n^{\prime}_{r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+1}\leq n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+1}
if
d_{0}\geq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+2
, and
n^{\prime}_{r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}}\leq n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}}
if
d_{0}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}-1
;

(iii)

\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
and
\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

The ‘only if’ part follows immediately from the definition of a
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted improvement, and it suffices to prove the ‘if’ part. It is harmless to replace
\Theta^{\prime}
with a
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted maximal tuple in its
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted equivalence class, and we need to show that
\Theta^{\prime}=\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}
. Item
(iii)
(which implies
(I^{\prime})^{d_{0}}\supseteq(I^{s_{0},d_{0}})^{d_{0}}
) together with
\Theta^{\prime}
being
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted maximal forces
\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
,
\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
and in particular
(I^{\prime})^{d_{0}}=(I^{s_{0},d_{0}})^{d_{0}}\subseteq I_{0}
. The fact that
\Theta^{\prime}
is
(s_{0},d_{0})
-twisted maximal also implies that
\max\{m\mid(m,\iota)\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{d}\}\geq 2n^{\prime}_{d}-2\#(I^{\prime})^{d}\cap I_{0}-1
for each
r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+2\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}-1
satisfying
d\neq d_{0}
, using arguments in the proof Lemma
2.16
. Using item
(i)
and Condition
2.30
, we have

(2.13)

n^{\prime}_{d}\leq\#(I^{\prime})^{d}\cap I_{0}+\frac{1}{2}(1+\max\{m\mid(m,\iota)\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{d}\})=n^{s_{0},d_{0}}-\#(I^{s_{0},d_{0}})^{d}\cap I_{0}+\#(I^{\prime})^{d}\cap I_{0}

for each
r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+2\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}-1
satisfying
d\neq d_{0}
. Item
(ii)
implies that (
2.13
) holds for
d\in\{r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+1,r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}\}\setminus\{d_{0}\}
as well. Sum up all the inequalities for
r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}
(with
n^{\prime}_{d_{0}}=n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d_{0}}
), we obtain
\sum_{d=r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+1}^{r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}}n^{\prime}_{d}\leq\sum_{d=r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+1}^{r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d}
which has to be an equality. Hence, all inequalities are equalities and we have
I^{\prime}=I^{s_{0},d_{0}}
and thus
\Theta^{\prime}=\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}
.
∎

Proposition 2.32
.

Let
\Theta=(v,I,\underline{k},\underline{\Lambda})
be a maximally
(I_{0},I_{1})
-atomic tuple and
\Omega
its equivalence class. Assume that Condition
2.29
holds for some
1\leq s_{0}\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
and thus
\Theta^{s_{0},d_{0}}
as well as
\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
are defined for each
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d_{0}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}}
. Then we have

x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}}-x_{\Omega}\in d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(E_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k})

for each
1\leq s_{0}\leq r_{I_{v}\cap I_{1}}
and
r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d_{0}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}}
.

Proof.

As
\Theta^{s_{0},r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+1}=\Theta
by definition, it suffices to assume that
d_{0}\geq r_{v,I_{1},I}^{s_{0}-1}+2
and prove that

x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}}-x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}-1}}\in d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(E_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}).

Let
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
be a tuple, and then set
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}\in(I_{v}\cap I_{1})\setminus I^{\prime}
. Then we write
\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
for the set of tuples of the form
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
for some
\Theta^{\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
, and define
x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}}\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime\prime})x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}
. We claim that

(2.14)

d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}})=\varepsilon(\Omega,s_{0},d_{0})(x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}}-x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}-1}})

for some
\varepsilon(\Omega,s_{0},d_{0})\in\{1,-1\}
, which is clearly sufficient for our purpose.

Let
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
and
\Theta^{\prime}=(v,I^{\prime},\underline{k}^{\prime},\underline{\Lambda}^{\prime})
be an arbitrary tuple satisfying

(2.15)

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{\Theta^{\prime\prime}}))\neq 0.

There exists a unique
1\leq d\leq r_{I^{\prime\prime}}
and
i\in(I^{\prime\prime})^{d}\setminus I^{\prime}
such that
\Theta^{\prime\prime}=p_{i}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
. Condition
2.29
on
\Omega
and the definition of
\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
imply that
\#\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{d^{\prime}}\leq 1
for each
1\leq d^{\prime}\leq r_{I^{\prime\prime}}
and in fact
\#\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=1
for each
r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d^{\prime}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime\prime}}^{s_{0}}
. Hence, we have the following two possibilities

•

\Lambda^{\prime}_{d}=\emptyset
,
\Lambda^{\prime}_{d+1}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{d}
and we set
d_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}d
;

•

\Lambda^{\prime}_{d}=\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{d}
,
\Lambda^{\prime}_{d+1}=\emptyset
and we set
d_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}d+1
.

In particular, we always have
\Lambda^{\prime}_{d_{1}}=\emptyset
and thus
d_{1}
is uniquely determined by
\Theta^{\prime}
(as the unique
r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1}+1\leq d^{\prime}\leq r_{v,I_{1},I^{\prime}}^{s_{0}}
satisfying
\Lambda^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=\emptyset
). Now we fix a tuple
\Theta^{\prime}
such that there exists
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
that satisfies (
2.15
), and then write
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{1}}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}
and
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{1}-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}
for short. We note that such
\Theta^{\prime\prime}
has to be either
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
or
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
. We clearly have
\varepsilon(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))=(-1)^{n^{\prime}_{d_{1}}}\varepsilon(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))
and

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))=(-1)^{n^{\prime}_{d_{1}}-1}c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})})).

We have the following observations for
\Theta^{\prime}
.

•

Any
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
satisfying (
2.15
) must be either
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
or
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})
, and at least one of them is in
\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
by our choice of
\Theta^{\prime}
.

•

If
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}),p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
, then we have

(2.16)

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}}))=\varepsilon(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))+\varepsilon(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))=0.

•

If
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
and
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\notin\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
, we have

(2.17)

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}}))=\varepsilon(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})})).

•

If
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\notin\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
and
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
, we have

(2.18)

c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}}))=\varepsilon(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})})).

•

If
d_{1}\notin\{d_{0}-1,d_{0}\}
, then we must have
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}),p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
.

We claim the following two facts

(i)

If
d_{1}=d_{0}-1
, then we always have
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
, and
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\notin\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
if and only if
\Theta^{\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}-1}
.

(ii)

If
d_{1}=d_{0}
, then we always have
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
, and
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\notin\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
if and only if
\Theta^{\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
.

We first treat item
(i)
, namely the case when
d_{1}=d_{0}-1
.
We note that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
(resp.
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
) forces
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
(resp.
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
), therefore we always have

\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{s_{0},d_{0}-1}_{d^{\prime}}.

So it follows from Lemma
2.31
(with
d_{0}
replacing with
d_{0}-1
) that
\Theta^{\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}-1}
if and only if

(2.19)

\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}-1}_{d^{\prime}}.

We have the following possibilities

•

If (
2.19
) holds, then we clearly deduce
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\notin\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
from the definition of
\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
(namely any
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
should satisfy
\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{\prime\prime}_{d^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{s_{0},d_{0}-1}_{d^{\prime}}
).

•

If (
2.19
) is false, then we deduce from
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\notin I_{0}
that

(2.20)

\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{s_{0},d_{0}-1}_{d^{\prime}}=\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-2}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}.

Assume on the contrary that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\notin\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
, then we must have
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
by the choice of
\Theta^{\prime}
, and thus there exists
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
such that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})
. But this together with (
2.20
) and Lemma
2.31
implies that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
and

p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}\left(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})\right)\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+},

a contradiction. Hence we deduce that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
and thus there exists
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
such that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime})
(with
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
and
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
). We consider the unique tuple
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
that satisfies
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime})
, which clearly satisfies
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}=i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
and
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
, and thus
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
by Lemma
2.31
. Consequently, we have
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
.

Now we treat item
(ii)
, namely the case when
d_{1}=d_{0}
.
We note that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
(resp.
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
) forces
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
(resp.
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
), therefore we always have

\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}.

So it follows from Lemma
2.31
that
\Theta^{\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
if and only if

(2.21)

\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}.

We have the following possibilities

•

If (
2.21
) holds, then we clearly deduce
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\notin\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
from the definition of
\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
(namely any
\Theta^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
should satisfy
\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{\prime\prime}_{d^{\prime}}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
).

•

If (
2.21
) is false, then we deduce from
i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}\notin I_{0}
that

(2.22)

\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{\prime}_{d^{\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}.

Assume on the contrary that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\notin\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
, then we must have
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
by the choice of
\Theta^{\prime}
, and thus there exists
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
such that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})
and
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
. But this together with (
2.22
) and Lemma
2.31
implies that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
and

p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}\left(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime})\right)\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+},

a contradiction. Hence we deduce that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
and thus there exists
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
such that
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime})
(with
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
and
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime}}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
). We consider the unique tuple
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}
that satisfies
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{-}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime})
, which clearly satisfies
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}=i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}-1}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
and
i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}=i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}\geq\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{d_{0}}n^{s_{0},d_{0}}_{d^{\prime}}
, and thus
\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0}}
by Lemma
2.31
. Consequently, we have
p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})=p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime})\in\Omega^{s_{0},d_{0},+}
.

Finally, we note that there exists a number
\varepsilon(\Omega,s_{0},d_{0})\in\{1,-1\}
depending only on
\Omega
,
s_{0}
and
d_{0}
such that, for each
\Theta^{\prime}
considered above, we have

\varepsilon(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))=-\varepsilon(\Omega,s_{0},d_{0})\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime})

if
d_{1}=d_{0}-1
, and

\varepsilon(p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime}))c_{\Theta^{\prime}}(d_{1,I_{0},I_{1}}^{-\ell-1,k}(x_{p_{i_{\Theta^{\prime}}^{\prime}}^{+}(\Theta^{\prime})}))=\varepsilon(\Omega,s_{0},d_{0})\varepsilon(\Theta^{\prime})

if
d_{1}=d_{0}
. This together with (
2.17
) and (
2.18
) finish the proof of (
2.14
).
∎

3.
A result on decomposition of locally analytic distributions

In this section, we prove a technical result on (tensor) decomposition of certain space of locally analytic distributions in Proposition
3.9
, which is essential for our computations of
N
-(co)homologies using Bruhat stratifications in Section
4.1
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
I
be a set and
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a projective system of locally convex
E
vector space and
W
another locally convex
E
vector space. Then we have a canonical topological isomorphism

(\varprojlim_{i}V_{i})\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\pi}W\cong\varprojlim_{i}(V_{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\pi}W).

If there exists a locally convex
E
-vector space
A
with separately continuous algebra structure such that
W
is a separately continuous left
A
-module and
V_{i}
is a separately continuous right
A
-module for each
i\in I
, then we have a canonical isomorphism

(\prod_{i}V_{i})\widehat{\otimes}_{A,\pi}W\cong\prod_{i}(V_{i}\widehat{\otimes}_{A,\pi}W).

Proof.

This is standard (using for example Corollary 17.9 of
[
S02
]
).
∎

According to Proposition 17.6 of
[
S02
]
, we can identify
V_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}V_{2}
and
V_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\pi}V_{2}
for two Frechét spaces
V_{1}
,
V_{2}
and sometimes write
V_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}V_{2}
for both of them. Note that a countable projective limit of Fréchet spaces is still Fréchet, but a countable inductive limit (in particular countable direct sum) of Fréchet spaces is in general not Fréchet. Consequently, the notation
V_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}V_{2}
would appear in the sequel typically when
V_{2}
is not Fréchet (say a countable direct sum of Fréchet spaces).

For each
p
-adic manifold
M
(paracompact and finite dimensional), we can consider the space of
E
-valued locally analytic functions
C^{\rm{an}}(M,E)
, whose strong dual is the space of distributions
D(M,E)
. For each closed subset
C\subseteq M
, we consider the closed subspace
D(M,E)_{C}\subseteq D(M,E)
consisting of distributions supported in
C
. If
C
is furthermore compact, we have an isomorphism
D(M,E)_{C}\cong(\varinjlim_{U}C^{\rm{an}}(U,E))^{\prime}
where
U
runs through open subset of
M
containing
C
. In particular, for each open subset
U\subseteq M
containing
C
, we have a canonical isomorphism
D(U,E)_{C}\cong D(M,E)_{C}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
M
be a
p
-adic manifold and
C_{1}\subseteq C_{2}
two compact subsets of
M
. Then we have a topological isomorphism

D(M,E)_{C_{2}}/D(M,E)_{C_{1}}\cong\varprojlim_{C}D(M,E)_{C}

where
C
runs through compact open subsets of
C_{2}\setminus C_{1}
.

Proof.

As
C
is compact open in
C_{2}
,
C_{2}\cong C\sqcup(C_{2}\setminus C)
(with disjoint union topology) and thus
D(M,E)_{C_{2}}\cong D(M,E)_{C}\oplus D(M,E)_{C_{2}\setminus C}
which induces a canonical projection
D(M,E)_{C_{2}}\twoheadrightarrow D(M,E)_{C}
with kernel
D(M,E)_{C_{2}\setminus C}\subseteq D(M,E)_{C_{2}}
. For each pair of compact open subsets
C\subseteq C^{\prime}
of
C_{2}\setminus C_{1}
, we have a canonical projection map
D(M,E)_{C^{\prime}}\cong D(M,E)_{C}\oplus D(M,E)_{C^{\prime}\setminus C}\twoheadrightarrow D(M,E)_{C}
, and thus we obtain a continuous map

D(M,E)_{C_{2}}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{C}D(M,E)_{C}
with dense image and kernel

\bigcap_{C}D(M,E)_{C_{2}\setminus C}
. As
C_{2}\setminus C_{1}
is a union of its compact open subsets, the intersection of
C_{2}\setminus C
for all possible compact open
C\subseteq C_{2}\setminus C_{1}
is exactly
C_{1}
, which implies that
\bigcap_{C}D(M,E)_{C_{2}\setminus C}=D(M,E)_{C_{1}}
. Now we prove the surjectivity of
D(M,E)_{C_{2}}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{C}D(M,E)_{C}
(which implies that
D(M,E)_{C_{2}}/D(M,E)_{C_{1}}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{C}D(M,E)_{C}
is a topological isomorphism). As
C_{2}
is compact, it suffices to replace
M
with a compact open neighborhood of
C_{2}
and thus assume that
M
is compact, which implies that
C^{\rm{an}}(M,E)=\varinjlim_{r}C^{\rm{an}}_{r}(M,E)
where
C^{\rm{an}}_{r}(M,E)\subseteq C^{\rm{an}}(M,E)
is the subspace consisting of locally analytic functions that are analytic on each open disc of radius
r
. By taking dual, we have
D(M,E)=\varprojlim_{r}D_{r}(M,E)
with
D_{r}(M,E)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}(C^{\rm{an}}_{r}(M,E))^{\prime}
. For each compact subset
C\subseteq M
, we write
D_{r}(M,E)_{C}
for the image of
D(M,E)_{C}\subseteq D(M,E)
in
D_{r}(M,E)
, then we have
D(M,E)_{C}\cong\varprojlim_{r}D_{r}(M,E)_{C}
. If we write
C_{r}
for the union of all (finite number of) open disc of radius
r
that intersect
C
non-trivially, then
C_{r}
is a compact open subset of
M
that contains
C
and satisfies
D_{r}(M,E)_{C}=D_{r}(M,E)_{C_{r}}
. Now let
C
be a compact open subset of
C_{2}\setminus C_{1}
. For each fixed
r
,
C_{r}
stablizes when
C
is sufficiently large, and thus
\varprojlim_{C}D_{r}(M,E)_{C}=D_{r}(M,E)_{C}
for some sufficiently large
C
. Hence, the canonical map
D_{r}(M,E)_{C_{2}}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{C}D_{r}(M,E)_{C}
is a continuous surjection (of Banach spaces). For each
0<r^{\prime}<r<1
,
D_{r^{\prime}}(M,E)_{C_{2}}\rightarrow D_{r}(M,E)_{C_{2}}
and
\varprojlim_{C}D_{r^{\prime}}(M,E)_{C}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{C}D_{r}(M,E)_{C}
are injective, and thus
\varprojlim_{r}D_{r}(M,E)_{C_{2}}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{r}\varprojlim_{C}D_{r}(M,E)_{C}
is a continuous surjection. Note that we clearly have
\varprojlim_{r}\varprojlim_{C}D_{r}(M,E)_{C}=\varprojlim_{C}\varprojlim_{r}D_{r}(M,E)_{C}=\varprojlim_{C}D(M,E)_{C}
, and the proof is thus finished.
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
M_{1},M_{2}
two
p
-adic manifolds with closed subsets
C_{1}\subseteq M_{1}
and
C_{2}\subseteq M_{2}
. Then we have a canonical isomorphism
D(M_{1}\times M_{2},E)_{C_{1}\times C_{2}}\cong D(M_{1},E)_{C_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(M_{2},E)_{C_{2}}
.

Proof.

This is Lemma 3.2.12 of
[
BD19
]
.
∎

Let
G_{/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
be a reductive group and
P_{0}\subseteq G
be a fixed minimal parabolic subgroup containing a fixed maximal split torus
L_{0}
. Let
P_{1},P_{2}\subseteq G
be two parabolic subgroups containing
P_{0}
. We can associate with
P_{1},P_{2}
the Bruhat stratification
\{P_{1}wP_{2}\}_{W^{P_{1},P_{2}}}
where
W^{P_{1},P_{2}}
is the corresponding set of minimal length representatives. We write
P_{1}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}w^{-1}P_{1}w
,
Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}P_{1}^{\prime}P_{2}\subseteq G
,
\overline{Y}
for the (reduced) Zariski closure of
Y
in
G
and
Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\overline{Y}\setminus Y
with the reduced scheme structure.
We write
N_{2}
for the unipotent radical of
P_{2}
and
L_{2}\subseteq P_{2}
its maximal Levi subgroup containing
L_{0}
. Then we set
L_{2}^{\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}L_{2}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}
and
N_{2}^{\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}N_{2}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}
. Note that

Y\cong P_{1}^{\prime}\times(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Y)\cong P_{1}^{\prime}\times(P_{1}^{\prime}\cap P_{2}\backslash P_{2})\cong P_{1}^{\prime}\times(L_{2}^{\flat}\backslash L_{2})\times(N_{2}^{\flat}\backslash N_{2})

and the isomorphisms preserve the natural left
P_{1}^{\prime}
-action and right
P_{2}
-action on each term. Note that
L_{2}^{\flat}
is a parabolic subgroup inside
L_{2}
, and thus
L_{2}^{\flat}\backslash L_{2}
is proper.

We write
D(G)_{\overline{Y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}D(G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),E)_{\overline{Y}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
for short and similarly for others (in particular, we omit
E
from all spaces of distributions). As
G
,
\overline{Y}
and
Z
are left
P_{1}^{\prime}
-stable and right
P_{2}
-stable,
D(G)_{\overline{Y}}
and
D(G)_{Z}
are naturally left
D(P_{2})
-modules and right
D(P_{1}^{\prime})
-modules. In the following, we will frequently abbreviate
P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
as
P_{1}^{\prime}
and the others are similar.

We set
\widehat{D}(G)_{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\left(\varprojlim_{C}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C}\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime})
where
C
running through all compact open subsets of
P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Y(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Lemma 3.4
.

The limit
\widehat{D}(G)_{Y}=\left(\varprojlim_{C}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C}\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime})
is canonically a left
D(P_{2})
-module and a right
D(P_{1}^{\prime})
-module.

Proof.

The right
D(P_{1}^{\prime})
-module structure is obvious from the definition of
\widehat{D}(G)_{Y}
.
Let
H_{2}\subseteq P_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be an arbitrary compact open subgroup. Then for each compact open subset
C\subseteq P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Y(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
CH_{2}\subseteq P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Y(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is an open closed subset (containing
C
) which is right
H_{2}
-stable. Consequently, right
H_{2}
-stable compact open subsets of
P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Y(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
are cofinal among all compact open subsets of
P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Y(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, which implies that
\varprojlim_{C}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C}
is canonically a left
D(H_{2})
-module. There clearly exist a discrete subset
J_{2}\subseteq P_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
P_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\bigsqcup_{g_{2}\in J_{2}}H_{2}g_{2}
as a
p
-adic manifold. For each compact open subset
C
of
P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Y(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which is right
H_{2}
-stable, we have canonical isomorphisms
\ell_{g_{2}^{-1}}:D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C}\rightarrow\delta_{g_{2}^{-1}}\ast D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}C}=D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C\cdot g_{2}}
. If we take inverse limit among all such
C
, we obtain the canonical left
D(P_{2})
-action on
\varprojlim_{C}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C}
and thus on
\widehat{D}(G)_{Y}=\left(\varprojlim_{C}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C}\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime})
as well.
∎

Proposition 3.5
.

There exists a canonical isomorphisms
D(G)_{\overline{Y}}/D(G)_{Z}\cong\widehat{D}(G)_{Y}
which respects the left
D(P_{2})
-action and right
D(P_{1}^{\prime})
-action.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.3
that

D(G)_{\overline{Y}}\cong D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash\overline{Y}}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime})\mbox{ and }D(G)_{Z}\cong D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Z}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime}),

and thus

(D(G)_{\overline{Y}}/D(G)_{Z}\cong\left(D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash\overline{Y}}/D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Z})\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime}).

Consequently, it remains to construct a canonical
D(P_{2})
-equivariant isomorphism

(3.1)

D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash\overline{Y}}/D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Z}\cong\varprojlim_{C}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C}

according to the definition of
\widehat{D}(G)_{Y}
. This follows from Lemma
3.2
as well as the facts that both
(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash\overline{Y})(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Z)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
are compact subsets of
(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
Now we check the left
D(P_{2})
-equivariance.
We borrow notation from the proof of Lemma
3.4
. If
C
is a right
H_{2}
-stable compact open subset of
P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Y(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, then the map
D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash\overline{Y}}/D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash Z}\rightarrow D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{C}
is clearly left
D(H_{2})
-equivariant (as the
D(H_{2})
-actions on both sides are compatible with that on
D(G)
). The extension to left
D(P_{2})
-equivariance of (
3.1
) follows from the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
3.4
.
∎

Let
G_{0}\subseteq G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be a compact open subgroup and choose
H_{1}\subseteq G_{0}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
H_{2}\subseteq G_{0}\cap P_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Then exactly the same proof as that of (4.87) of
[
Schr11
]
shows that we have a canonical topological isomorphism (with all the involved spaces being Fréchet)

(3.2)

D(G_{0})_{H_{1}H_{2}}\cong D(H_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2})_{H_{2}\cap H_{1}}}D(G_{0})_{H_{1}}.

It is clearly harmless to replace
G_{0}
with
G
as it does not change the corresponding space of distributions. So we drop
G_{0}
from now on and consider compact open subgroups
H_{1}\subseteq P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
H_{2}\subseteq P_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We fix discrete subset
J_{1}\subseteq P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
J_{2}\subseteq P_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\bigsqcup_{g_{1}\in J_{1}}g_{1}H_{1}
and
P_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\bigsqcup_{g_{2}\in J_{2}}H_{2}g_{2}
as
p
-adic manifolds.
We set
H_{2}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}H_{2}\cap L_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}H_{2}\cap N_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Definition 3.6
.

We say the pair
(H_{1},H_{2})
is
good
if

(i)

H_{2}=H_{2}^{\prime}H_{2}^{\prime\prime}
,
H_{2}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=H_{2}\cap H_{1}=(H_{2}^{\prime}\cap H_{1})(H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap H_{1})
;

(ii)

(H_{2}^{\prime}\cap H_{1})\backslash H_{2}^{\prime}\cong L_{2}^{\flat}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\backslash L_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
; and

(iii)

P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})H_{2}=\bigsqcup_{g_{1}\in J_{1}}g_{1}H_{1}H_{2}
.

Item
(i)
clearly implies topological isomorphisms (of Fréchet spaces)
D(H_{2})\cong D(H_{2}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{E}D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})
and
D(H_{2})_{H_{2}\cap H_{1}}\cong D(H_{2}^{\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime}\cap H_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap H_{1}}
with the convolution having the form induced from semi-direct product. These topological isomorphisms together with (
3.2
) implies that

D(G)_{H_{1}H_{2}}\cong D(H_{2}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime}\cap H_{1}}}(D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap H_{1}}}D(G)_{H_{1}}).

By convolution with
\delta_{g_{1}^{-1}}
from the right, we obtain

D(G)_{g_{1}H_{1}H_{2}}\cong D(H_{2}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime}\cap H_{1}}}(D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap H_{1}}}D(G)_{g_{1}H_{1}}).

for each
g_{1}\in P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. As
P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is closed in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
H_{2}
is compact,
P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})H_{2}
is closed in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. By taking direct sum over all
g_{1}\in J_{1}
and using item
(iii)
of Definition
3.6
(and the fact that
\widehat{\otimes}_{\iota}
commutes with direct sum), we obtain (by abusing
P_{1}^{\prime}
for
P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
)

D(G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}H_{2}}\cong D(H_{2}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}}}(D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}}}D(G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}}).

According to item
(ii)
of Definition
3.6
, we can replace
D(H_{2}^{\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime}\cap H_{1}}}
with
D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}}}
and deduce

(3.3)

D(G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}H_{2}}\cong D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}}}(D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}}}D(G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}}).

(Note that
L_{2}^{\flat}\backslash L_{2}
is compact and thus we do not distinguish between
D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}},\iota}
with
D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}},\pi}
.)
By factoring out
\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime})
from both sides of (
3.3
), we obtain the following isomorphism (with
D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash P_{1}^{\prime}H_{2}}
,
D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})
and
D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1}
being Fréchet)

(3.4)

D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash P_{1}^{\prime}H_{2}}\cong D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}}}(D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1}).

We have the following two observations

•

If
(H_{1},H_{2})
is a good pair, then
(gH_{1}g^{-1},gH_{2}g^{-1})
is a good pair for any
g\in L_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

•

For each compact open subgroup
H\subseteq N_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we have
N_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\bigcup_{g\in L_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}gHg^{-1}
. In particular
\{gHg^{-1}\}_{g\in L_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
is cofinal among all compact open subgroups of
N_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
under inclusion.

Consequently, if we take (countable) projective limit with respect all possible
H_{2}
that appears in a good pair
(H_{1},H_{2})
(and then use Lemma
3.1
for
D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}},\pi}
), we conclude that

(3.5)

\varprojlim_{H_{2}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash P_{1}^{\prime}H_{2}}\cong D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}}}\left(\varprojlim_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}}D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1}\right).

We have
H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}=H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap N_{2}^{\flat}
,
(H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap N_{2}^{\flat})\backslash H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cong N_{2}^{\flat}\backslash N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime}
, as well as canonical topological isomorphisms

D(N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\cong D(N_{2}^{\flat}\backslash N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(N_{2}^{\flat})

and

D(N_{2}^{\flat}\backslash N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(N_{2}^{\flat})\cong D(N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{N_{2}^{\flat}}\cong D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}}\cong D(N_{2}^{\flat}\backslash N_{2})_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(N_{2}^{\flat}).

Note that for each Fréchet space
V
and
\ast\in\{\pi,\iota\}
,
D(N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}},\ast}V
is a quotient of
D(N_{2}^{\flat}\backslash N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\ast}V
by a closed subspace with
D(N_{2}^{\flat}\backslash N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})
being Fréchet, so we do not distinguish between
D(N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}},\pi}
and
D(N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}},\iota}
.
Consequently, we have a topological isomorphism

D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1}\cong D(N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1}

for each
H_{2}
that appears in a good pair
(H_{1},H_{2})
,
which implies that (using Lemma
3.1
)

\varprojlim_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}}D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H_{2}^{\prime\prime})_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1}\cong\varprojlim_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}}D(N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1}\cong\widehat{D}(N_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1}

where
\widehat{D}(N_{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\left(\varprojlim_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}}D(N_{2}^{\flat}\backslash N_{2}^{\flat}H_{2}^{\prime\prime})\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(N_{2}^{\flat})
. This together with (
3.5
) implies that

(3.6)

\widehat{D}(G)_{Y}\cong D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}}}(\widehat{D}(N_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1})\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime}).

Now we consider the following condition

Condition 3.7
.

There exists a
p
-adic field
K
, a split reductive group
\widetilde{G}_{/L}
together with its two parabolic subgroups
\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{P}_{2}
such that
G\cong\mathrm{Res}_{K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\widetilde{G})
,
P_{1}^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{Res}_{K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime})
and
P_{2}\cong\mathrm{Res}_{K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\widetilde{P}_{2})
.

Lemma 3.8
.

If Condition
3.7
holds, then a good pair
(H_{1},H_{2})
exists.

Proof.

We fix
K
,
\widetilde{G}
,
\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{P}_{2}
as in Condition
3.7
. As
\widetilde{G}
is split, we abuse the same notation for the Chevalley group scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, where
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is the ring of integers of
K
. Similarly,
\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{P}_{2}
can be made closed subgroup schemes of
\widetilde{G}_{/\mathcal{O}_{K}}
. Let
\widetilde{L}_{0}\subseteq\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime}\cap\widetilde{P}_{2}
be a (split) maximal torus which always exists. Upon replacing
L_{0}
with
\mathrm{Res}_{K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widetilde{L}_{0}
, we may assume that
L_{0}\cong\mathrm{Res}_{K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\widetilde{L}_{0}
and moreover
\widetilde{L}_{0}
extends to a closed subgroup scheme of
\widetilde{G}_{/\mathcal{O}_{K}}
. Given the pair
(\widetilde{G},\widetilde{L}_{0})
, we consider the corresponding set of roots
\Phi
, and
\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime}
(resp.
\widetilde{P}_{2}
) corresponds to a (closed) subset
\Phi_{1}
(resp.
\Phi_{2}
) of
\Phi
. We write
\widetilde{N}_{2}
for the unipotent radical of
\widetilde{P}_{2}
and there exists a unique maximal Levi subgroup
\widetilde{L}_{2}\subseteq\widetilde{P}_{2}
containing
\widetilde{L}_{0}
, such that
\widetilde{P}_{2}=\widetilde{L}_{2}\widetilde{N}_{2}
is a semi direct product. In the following, we understand all the group schemes mentioned above to be over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. The closed subgroup
\widetilde{L}_{2}
(resp.
\widetilde{N}_{2}
) of
\widetilde{P}_{2}
corresponds to a closed subset
\Phi_{2}^{\prime}
(resp.
\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}
) of
\Phi_{2}
such that
\Phi_{2}=\Phi_{2}^{\prime}\sqcup\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}
. The closed subsets
\Phi_{1}\cap\Phi_{2}
,
\Phi_{1}\cap\Phi_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{1}\cap\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}
of
\Phi_{2}
corresponds to closed subgroup schemes
\widetilde{P}_{1}\cap\widetilde{P}_{2}
,
\widetilde{P}_{1}\cap\widetilde{L}_{2}
and
\widetilde{P}_{1}\cap\widetilde{N}_{2}
such that
\widetilde{P}_{1}\cap\widetilde{P}_{2}=(\widetilde{P}_{1}\cap\widetilde{L}_{2})(\widetilde{P}_{1}\cap\widetilde{N}_{2})
is a semi direct product. Now we claim that
H_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
and
H_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\widetilde{P}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
gives a good pair as in Definition
3.6
. As we clearly have
H_{2}^{\prime}=\widetilde{L}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
and
H_{2}^{\prime\prime}=\widetilde{N}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, item
(i)
follows from the corresponding facts on group schemes, and the fact that

H_{2}\cap P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\widetilde{P}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})\cap\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime}(L)=\widetilde{P}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{K})\cap(\widetilde{P}_{2}\cap\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime})(L)=(\widetilde{P}_{2}\cap\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime})(\mathcal{O}_{K}),

as
\widetilde{P}_{2}\cap\widetilde{P}_{1}^{\prime}
is a closed subgroup scheme of
\widetilde{P}_{2}
. Item
(ii)
follows from the fact that
\widetilde{L}_{2}\cap\widetilde{P}_{1}\backslash\widetilde{L}_{2}
is proper over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. For item
(iii)
, we clearly have
P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})H_{2}=\bigcup_{g_{1}\in J_{1}}g_{1}H_{1}H_{2}
. Let
g_{1},g_{1}^{\prime}\in J_{1}
be elements such that
g_{1}H_{1}H_{2}\cap g_{1}^{\prime}H_{1}H_{2}\neq\emptyset
, namely there exists
h_{1},h_{1}^{\prime}\in H_{1}
and
h_{2},h_{2}^{\prime}\in H_{2}
such that
g_{1}h_{1}h_{2}=g_{1}^{\prime}h_{1}^{\prime}h_{2}^{\prime}
. As we have a natural bijection
P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\backslash P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})H_{2}\cong(P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap H_{2})\backslash H_{2}=(H_{1}\cap H_{2})\backslash H_{2}
,
P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})h_{2}=P_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})h_{2}^{\prime}
implies that
h_{2}^{\prime}h_{2}^{-1}\in H_{1}\cap H_{2}
, which together with
g_{1}h_{1}h_{2}=g_{1}^{\prime}h_{1}^{\prime}h_{2}^{\prime}
forces
g_{1}H_{1}=g_{1}^{\prime}H_{1}
and thus
g_{1}=g_{1}^{\prime}
by the definition of
J_{1}
. The proof is thus finished.
∎

Proposition 3.9
.

If Condition
3.7
holds, then we have

D(G)_{\overline{Y}}/D(G)_{Z}\cong\widehat{D}(G)_{Y}\cong D(L_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{2})_{L_{2}^{\flat}}}(\widehat{D}(N_{2})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{2})_{N_{2}^{\flat}}}D(P_{1}^{\prime}\backslash G)_{1})\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{1}^{\prime}).

Proof.

This follows directly from Proposition
3.5
, (
3.6
) and Lemma
3.8
.
∎

4.
Extensions between locally analytic generalized Steinberg

In this section, we compute various
\mathrm{Ext}
-groups between locally analytic generalized Steinberg representations ( see Theorem
4.22
and Corollary
4.23
). Based on computations of the spectral sequence
E_{\bullet,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
in Section
2
and the decomposition in Section
3
(see Proposition
3.9
), the main extra technical ingredients are the computations of some
N
-(co)homologies (see Lemma
4.14
and Lemma
4.15
) which, via a standard spectral sequence, compute the
\mathrm{Ext}
-groups between locally analytic principal series in Proposition
4.16
.

4.1.
Locally analytic geometric lemma

In this section, we generalize the classical Bernstein–Zelevinsky geometric lemma in smooth representation theory (see Section 2.12 of
[
BZ77
]
) to Orlik-Strauch representations in Proposition
4.7
. As an application, we compute the
\mathrm{Ext}
-groups between various
i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
in Proposition
4.16
. We write
N_{n,I}
for the unipotent radical of
P_{n,I}
, for each
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
.

Let
I,I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be two subsets, and we write
W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}\subseteq W(G_{n})
be a set of (minimal length) representative so that
\{P_{n,I^{\prime}}wP_{n,I}\}_{w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}}
is a stratification on the group scheme
G_{n}
. For each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, we write
C_{w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}P_{n,I^{\prime}}wP_{n,I}
(which is a locally closed subscheme of
G_{n}
) and
\overline{C_{w}}
for its Zariski closure in
G_{n}
. We will abuse
C_{w}
and
\overline{C_{w}}
for their
K
-points from now on. As in Section
3
, we use the shorted notation
D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}D(G_{n},E)_{\overline{C_{w}}}
and similarly for other spaces of distributions. Then
\{D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}}\}_{w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}}
is an increasing
W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
-filtration on
D(G_{n})
with respect to the Bruhat order on
W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
. As
\overline{C_{w}}\setminus C_{w}=\bigcup_{w^{\prime}\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I},w^{\prime}<w}\overline{C_{w^{\prime}}}
, we clearly have

D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}\setminus C_{w}}=\sum_{w^{\prime}\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I},w^{\prime}<w}D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w^{\prime}}}},

and thus
D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}}/D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}\setminus C_{w}}
is the
w
-graded piece associated with the filtration
\{D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}}\}_{w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}}
. For technical convenience, we set
P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}w^{-1}P_{n,I^{\prime}}w
,
L_{n,I,w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}
,
N_{n,I,w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}N_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}
,
Y_{w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}w^{-1}C_{w}
whose closure in
G_{n}
is
\overline{Y_{w}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}w^{-1}\overline{C_{w}}
. According to Proposition
3.5
, there exists a canonical left
D(P_{n,I})
-equivariant and right
D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w})
-equivariant isomorphism
