\widehat{D}(G_{n})_{Y_{w}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\varprojlim_{C}D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}\backslash G_{n})_{C}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w})\cong D(G_{n})_{\overline{Y_{w}}}/D(G_{n})_{\overline{Y_{w}}\setminus Y_{w}}

where
C
runs through compact open subsets of
P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}\backslash P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}P_{n,I}
.

Given a coadmissible
D(L_{n,I^{\prime}})
-module
\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}}
and an integer
k\geq 0
, it is a fundamental question to compute

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M})

with
\mathcal{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}D(G_{n})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}),\iota}\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}}
.
With the help of the filtration
\{D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}}\}_{w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}}
, we usually first compute

(4.1)

H^{k}(N_{n,I},(D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}}/D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}\setminus C_{w}})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}),\iota}\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}})

for each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
. We clearly have a canonical isomorphism

(4.2)

\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}(D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}}/D(G_{n})_{\overline{C_{w}}\setminus C_{w}})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}),\iota}\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}}\cong(D(G_{n})_{\overline{Y_{w}}}/D(G_{n})_{\overline{Y_{w}}\setminus Y_{w}})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}),\iota}\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}}^{w}

with
\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}}^{w}
being the
w
-conjugate of
\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}}
. Combine with Proposition
3.9
, (
4.1
) can be reduced to

(4.3)

H^{k}(N_{n,I},D(L_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}}(\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}}D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}\backslash G_{n})_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}}^{w}))

for each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, where
\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\left(\varprojlim_{C}D(C)\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D(N_{n,I,w})
and
C
runs through compact open subsets of
N_{n,I,w}\backslash N_{n,I}
. See for example the discussion after Lemme 5.1.1 of
[
Bre19
]
that (
4.3
) admits a natural
D(L_{n,I})
-module structure (and in particular a
D(Z_{n,I})
-module structure).

We assume from now on that
\mathcal{M}_{I^{\prime}}=L^{I^{\prime}}(\mu)\otimes_{E}(\pi^{\infty})^{\prime}
with
\mu\in X(T_{n,E})
dominant with respect to
B_{n,E}\cap L_{n,I^{\prime},E}
and
\pi^{\infty}
an admissible smooth representation of
L_{n,I^{\prime}}
.
Now we set

\mathcal{M}^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}\backslash G_{n})_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}L^{I^{\prime}}(\mu)\cong D(G_{n})_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{D(P_{n,I^{\prime}})_{1}}L^{I^{\prime}}(\mu)

\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}\cong D(G_{n})_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w})_{1}}(L^{I^{\prime}}(\mu)^{w}\otimes_{E}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}),

\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}}\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M})

and note that

\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M})\cong D(L_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}}\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M})

for each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
.

Let
\mathfrak{p}
be a (finite dimensional) Lie algebra over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with an ideal
\mathfrak{n}
and a commutative sub Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}
such that
\mathfrak{t}\subseteq\mathfrak{p}
induces an embedding
\mathfrak{t}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{p}/\mathfrak{n}
. We set
\mathfrak{p}_{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\mathfrak{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}E
and similarly for others. We write
X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})
for the set of all eigencharacters of
\mathfrak{t}_{E}
(which is countable).

Definition 4.1
.

Let
V
be a Fréchet unclear space equipped with a
U(\mathfrak{p}_{E})
-action. We say the topological
U(\mathfrak{p}_{E})
-module
V
is
nice
if there exists a dense sub
U(\mathfrak{p}_{E})
-module
V^{\flat}\subseteq V
such that the
\mathfrak{t}_{E}
-eigenspace
V^{\flat}_{\eta}
is finite dimensional for each
\eta\in X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})
and
V^{\flat}=\bigoplus_{\eta\in X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})}V^{\flat}_{\eta}
. Here
V^{\flat}_{\eta}
for the
\eta
-eigenspace of
V^{\flat}
under the
\mathfrak{t}_{E}
-action.

We endow
V^{\flat}
with the subspace topology induced from
V
, and set
V^{\flat}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\bigoplus_{\eta\in X}V^{\flat}_{\eta}
for each subset
X\subseteq X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})
. Now we consider the Chevalley-Eilenberg complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V)
that computes the Lie algebra cohomology
H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{n}_{E},V)
. Equipped with its natural topology induced from
V
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V)
contains a (dense) subcomplex
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat})
with each term endowed with the induced subspace topology. Then there exists a finite set
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})
depending only on our choice of
\mathfrak{p}
, such that the image of
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat}_{X})
under

(4.4)

\varphi^{k}:~\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k+1}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat})

is contained in
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k+1}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat}_{X+\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}})
for each finite subset
X\subseteq X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})
(with
X+\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\{\eta+\eta^{\prime}\mid\eta\in X,\eta^{\prime}\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}\}
).

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
V
be a nice topological
U(\mathfrak{p}_{E})
-module as in Definition
4.1
. Each map of the complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat})
is strict with closed image, with respect to the topology induced from
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V)
.

Proof.

Let
k\geq 0
and consider the map (
4.4
). Let

X_{0}\subseteq X_{1}\subseteq\cdots

be a sequence of subsets of
X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})
satisfying
X_{i}+\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq X_{i+1}
for each
i\geq 0
and
X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})=\bigcup_{i=0}^{\infty}X_{i}
. As
V^{\flat}=\bigoplus_{\eta\in X(\mathfrak{t}_{E})}V^{\flat}_{\eta}
, we have
V^{\flat}=\varinjlim_{i}V^{\flat}_{X_{i}}
. By the definition of
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the map
\varphi^{k}
has the form
\varphi^{k}=\varinjlim_{i}\varphi^{k}_{i}
where
\varphi^{k}_{i}:\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat}_{X_{i}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k+1}\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat}_{X_{i+1}})
is the restriction of
\varphi^{k}
. As
\varphi^{k}_{i}
is a map between finite dimensional
E
-vector spaces for each
i\geq 0
, we deduce that
\varphi^{k}
is strict with closed image.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
V
be a nice topological
U(\mathfrak{p}_{E})
-module as in Definition
4.1
. For each
k\geq 0
, the space
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{E},V)
is a Fréchet nuclear space and the map
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{E},V^{\flat})\rightarrow H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{E},V)
is an injection with dense image.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
4.2
and Theorem 7.4 of
[
Koh11
]
.
∎

For each
p
-adic manifold
M
(paracompact and finite dimensional), we write
C^{\infty}(M)=C^{\infty}(M,E)
for the space of locally constant
E
-valued functions on
M
and set
D^{\infty}(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}C^{\infty}(M)^{\prime}
. The closed embedding
C^{\infty}(M)\hookrightarrow C^{\rm{an}}(M)
induces a canonical surjection

(4.5)

D(M)\twoheadrightarrow D^{\infty}(M).

For each closed subset
C\subseteq M
, we write
D^{\infty}(M)_{C}\subseteq D^{\infty}(M)
for the closed subspace consists of locally constant distributions that are supported on
C
, and note that
D^{\infty}(M)_{C}
is simply the image of
D(M)_{C}
under (
4.5
). If
C
is a closed submanifold of
M
, then we actually have a canonical isomorphism

D^{\infty}(M)_{C}\cong D^{\infty}(C):\delta\mapsto\delta|_{C}

which makes
D^{\infty}(M)_{C}
a much simpler object than
D(M)_{C}
. Lemma
3.2
and Lemma
3.3
have their analogue for
D^{\infty}(-)
, and
D^{\infty}(M)
is Fréchet nuclear if
M
is compact. In particular, we have a topological isomorphism

D^{\infty}(N_{n,I})\cong D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w}\backslash N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w}).

We set
\widehat{D}^{\infty}(N_{n,I})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}(\varprojlim_{C}D^{\infty}(C))\widehat{\otimes}_{E,\iota}D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w})
where
C
runs through compact open subsets of
N_{n,I,w}\backslash N_{n,I}
. Note that there exists a canonical
D(N_{n,I})
-equivariant surjection

\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{D}^{\infty}(N_{n,I})

with the
D(N_{n,I})
-action on the target factoring through
D^{\infty}(N_{n,I})
.

Lemma 4.4
.

For each
k\geq 0
and each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, the cohomology groups

H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w}),H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M})),~H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))

are Fréchet nuclear spaces. Moreover, we have canonical
D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}
-equivariant and
D^{\infty}(N_{n,I})
-equivariant topological isomorphisms

H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\widehat{D}^{\infty}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w})}H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M}))\\
\cong\left(\widehat{D}^{\infty}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w})}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}\right)\widehat{\otimes}_{E}H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w}).

Proof.

We fix an element
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
throughout.
As
(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w}\cong D(G_{n})_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{D(P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w})_{1}}L^{I^{\prime}}(\mu)^{w}
, we can consider the dense subspace
(\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat})^{w}
with

\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}U(\mathfrak{g}_{n,E})\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{p}_{n,I^{\prime},E})}L^{I^{\prime}}(\mu)

and observe that
(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w}
is a nice
U(\mathfrak{p}_{n,I,E})
-module (see Definition
4.1
). Therefore it follows from Lemma
4.2
(with
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{n,I}
,
\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{n}_{n,I}
and
\mathfrak{t}=\mathfrak{t}_{n}
) that each map of the complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w})
is strict with closed image and
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w})
is a Fréchet nuclear space for each
k\geq 0
. As
\pi^{\infty}
is admissible smooth, it is a countable direct limit of finite dimensional subspaces, making
(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}
a countable inverse limit of finite dimensional
E
-vector spaces. In particular,
(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}
is Fréchet nuclear and for each strict exact sequence of Fréchet nuclear spaces
V_{1}\hookrightarrow V_{2}\twoheadrightarrow V_{3}
, we have a strict exact sequence of Fréchet nuclear spaces

V_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}\hookrightarrow V_{2}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow V_{3}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}.

Consequently, each map of the complex

\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w})\widehat{\otimes}_{E}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}

is strict with closed image, making
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M}))
a Fréchet nuclear space for each
k\geq 0
.

Now we consider
D(N_{n,I})
-equivariant topological isomorphism of complexes

(4.6)

\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}}\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M})).

For each Fréchet nuclear space
V
we have the following topological isomorphism of Fréchet nuclear spaces

(4.7)

\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}}V\cong\varprojlim_{C,r}D(N_{n,I,w}C)_{r}\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I,w}C)_{N_{n,I,w},r}}V

with the radius
\frac{1}{p}<r<1
and
C
running through compact open subgroups of
N_{n,I}
. Following the argument of Lemme 4.27 of
[
Schr11
]
, we observe that
D(N_{n,I,w}C)_{r}
is finite free over
D(N_{n,I,w}C)_{N_{n,I,w},r}
and that the system

\{D(N_{n,I,w}C)_{r}\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I,w}C)_{N_{n,I,w},r}}V\}_{C,r}

is Mittag-Leffler. Consequently, for each strict exact sequence of Fréchet nuclear spaces
V_{1}\hookrightarrow V_{2}\twoheadrightarrow V_{3}
, we have a strict exact sequence of Fréchet nuclear spaces

\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}}V_{1}\hookrightarrow\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}}V_{2}\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}}V_{3}.

In particular, we deduces that each map of the complex (
4.6
) is strict with closed image, and
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))
is a Fréchet nuclear space for each
k\geq 0
. The above argument clearly produces a
D(N_{n,I})
-equivariant isomorphism

(4.8)

H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\widehat{D}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}}H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M}))

for each
k\geq 0
. As
\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E}
acts trivially on
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M}))
, the
D(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}
-action on
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M}))
factors through
D^{\infty}(N_{n,I})_{N_{n,I,w}}\cong D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w})
, and thus the
D(N_{n,I})
-equivariant isomorphism (
4.8
) factors through a
D^{\infty}(N_{n,I})
-equivariant isomorphism

(4.9)

H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\widehat{D}^{\infty}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w})}H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M}))

Then we observe that we have a
D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w})
-equivariant isomorphism

(4.10)

H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M}))\cong H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w})\widehat{\otimes}_{E}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}

for each
k\geq 0
. The
D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}
-equivariance of both (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) are clear.
∎

Note that we have a canonical isomorphism

\widehat{D}^{\infty}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w})}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}\cong\left((\rm{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Ind}_{N_{n,I,w}}^{N_{n,I}}\pi^{\infty,w})^{\infty}\right)^{\prime}

by the definition of the smooth compact induction functor
(\rm{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Ind}_{N_{n,I,w}}^{N_{n,I}}-)^{\infty}
and that of
\widehat{D}^{\infty}(N_{n,I})
.

Lemma 4.5
.

For each
k\geq 0
and each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, the cohomology group
H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))

is a Fréchet nuclear space, and we have a canonical
D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}
-equivariant topological isomorphism

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))\cong J_{n,I,I^{\prime},w}(\pi^{\infty})^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w}).

with
J_{n,I,I^{\prime},w}(\pi^{\infty})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\left((\rm{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Ind}_{N_{n,I,w}}^{N_{n,I}}\pi^{\infty,w})^{\infty}\right)_{N_{n,I}}
an admissible smooth
E
-representation of the Levi quotient of
L_{n,I,w}
.

Proof.

This follows directly from Lemma
4.4
and Theorem 7.1 of
[
Koh11
]
, namely the
D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}
-equivariant isomorphism

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))^{\prime}\cong\left(H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))^{\prime}\right)_{N_{n,I}}.

∎

Lemma 4.6
.

For each
k\geq 0
and each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, the cohomology groups
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))
and
H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))
are Fréchet nuclear spaces. Moreover, we have a canonical
D(L_{n,I})
-equivariant topological isomorphisms

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))\cong D(L_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}}H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M})).

Proof.

The same argument as Lemme 4.27 of
[
Schr11
]
shows that
D(L_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}}
sends a strict complex of Fréchet spaces to a strict complex of Fréchet spaces, and in particular

H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))\cong D(L_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}}H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))

for each
k\geq 0
, using the isomorphism of complexes

\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))\cong D(L_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}}\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{\bullet}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M})).

The desired isomorphism thus follows from Theorem 7.1 of
[
Koh11
]
, namely the
D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}
-equivariant isomorphism

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))^{\prime}\cong\left(H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M}))^{\prime}\right)_{N_{n,I}}

and the
D(L_{n,I})
-equivariant isomorphism

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))^{\prime}\cong\left(H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))^{\prime}\right)_{N_{n,I}}.

∎

Given a split reductive group
G
over
K
and a parabolic subgroup
P
with Levi quotient
L
, we recall from
[
OS15
]
the so-called Orlik–Strauch functor
\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(\cdot,\cdot)
which sends a pair
(V,\pi^{\infty})
to an admissible locally analytic representation
\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(V,\pi^{\infty})
of
G(K)
. Here
V
is a locally
\mathfrak{p}
-finite object in category
\mathcal{O}
and
\pi^{\infty}
is an admissible smooth
E
-representation of
L(K)
. The main properties of
\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(\cdot,\cdot)
are summarized in the main theorem of
loc.it.
and in particular
\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(\cdot,\cdot)
is exact in both arguments.

Combining all the results above, we arrive at the following locally analytic geometric lemma (with the terminology borrowed from the classical geometric lemma in smooth representation theory).

Proposition 4.7
.

For each
k\geq 0
and each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, we have a
D(L_{n,I})
-equivariant isomorphism of Fréchet nuclear spaces

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\mathcal{F}_{L_{n,I,w}}^{L_{n,I}}(H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat})^{w}),J_{n,I,I^{\prime},w}(\pi^{\infty}))^{\prime}.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat}
is
\mathfrak{t}_{n,E}
-semisimple and locally
\mathfrak{p}_{n,I^{\prime},E}
-finite,
(\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat})^{w}
is
\mathfrak{t}_{n,E}
-semisimple and locally
\mathfrak{p}_{n,I^{\prime},E}^{w}
-finite, making
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat})^{w})

\mathfrak{t}_{n,E}
-semisimple and locally
\mathfrak{l}_{n,I,w}=\mathfrak{l}_{n,I,E}\cap\mathfrak{p}_{n,I^{\prime},E}^{w}
-finite, and thus the Orlik-Strauch representation
\mathcal{F}_{L_{n,I,w}}^{L_{n,I}}(H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat})^{w}),J_{n,I,I^{\prime},w}(\pi^{\infty}))
is well-defined. We conclude by Lemma
4.5
, Lemma
4.6
and the following
D(L_{n,I})
-equivariant isomorphism

\mathcal{F}_{L_{n,I,w}}^{L_{n,I}}(H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat})^{w}),J_{n,I,I^{\prime},w}(\pi^{\infty}))^{\prime}\cong D(L_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(L_{n,I})_{L_{n,I,w}}}\left(J_{n,I,I^{\prime},w}(\pi^{\infty})^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w})\right).

∎

For each locally algebraic character
\chi
of
Z_{n,I}
and each
D(Z_{n,I})
-module
V
, we write
V_{\chi^{\prime}}
for its maximal quotient on which
D(Z_{n,I})
acts by
\chi^{\prime}
.
There exists a finite set
\Sigma_{w,k}(\mathcal{M})
of locally algebraic characters of
Z_{n,I}
such that the
\chi^{\prime}
-isotypic component
H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))_{\chi^{\prime}}
is non-zero for some locally algebraic character
\chi
of
Z_{n,I}
if and only if
\chi\in\Sigma_{w,k}(\mathcal{M})
, and moreover

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\bigoplus_{\chi\in\Sigma_{w,k}(\mathcal{M})}H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))_{\chi^{\prime}}.

Similarly, we write
\Sigma_{w}^{\infty}(\pi^{\infty})
for the set of smooth characters of
Z_{n,I}
such that
J_{n,I,I^{\prime},w}(\pi^{\infty})_{\chi^{\infty}}\neq 0
if and only if
\chi^{\infty}\in\Sigma_{w}^{\infty}(\pi^{\infty})
. There clearly exists a natural surjection
\Sigma_{w,k}(\mathcal{M})\twoheadrightarrow\Sigma_{w}^{\infty}(\pi^{\infty})
sending each
\chi\in\Sigma_{w,k}
to its smooth part
\chi^{\infty}
.

Definition 4.8
.

Let
I,I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be a pair of subsets and
\pi^{\infty}
an admissible smooth
E
-representation of
L_{n,I^{\prime}}
. We say that
\pi^{\infty}
is
(I,I^{\prime})
-regular
if
\Sigma_{w}\cap\Sigma_{w^{\prime}}=\emptyset
for each
w\neq w^{\prime}\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
.

We have the following corollary of Proposition
4.7

Corollary 4.9
.

Let
I,I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be a pair of subsets and
\pi^{\infty}
a
(I,I^{\prime})
-regular admissible smooth
E
-representation of
L_{n,I^{\prime}}
.
For each
k\geq 0
, the cohomology
H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M})
is a Fréchet nuclear space and there exists a
D(L_{n,I})
-equivariant isomorphism

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M})\cong\bigoplus_{w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}}H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))

Proof.

Note that
\mathcal{M}
admits a canonical filtration
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{w}(\mathcal{M})\}_{w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}}
. Our assumption implies that, for each smooth character
\chi^{\infty}
of
Z_{n,I}
, there exists at most one
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
such that

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))_{\chi^{\prime}}\neq 0

for some locally algebraic character
\chi
of
Z_{n,I}
whose smooth part is
\chi^{\infty}
.

We prove by induction on
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
with respect to Bruhat order.
For each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, we set
\mathrm{Fil}_{<w}(\mathcal{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{w^{\prime}<w}\mathrm{Fil}_{w^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M})
and consider the following long exact sequence

\cdots\rightarrow H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{<w}(\mathcal{M}))\rightarrow H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))\\
\rightarrow H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))\rightarrow H^{k+1}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{<w}(\mathcal{M}))\rightarrow\cdots

induced from
\mathrm{Fil}_{<w}(\mathcal{M})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fil}_{w}(\mathcal{M})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M})
. Our inductive assumption says that

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{w^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\bigoplus_{w^{\prime\prime}\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I},w^{\prime\prime}\leq w^{\prime}}H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w^{\prime\prime}}(\mathcal{M}))

for each
w^{\prime}<w
and
k\geq 0
, which implies that

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{Fil}_{<w}(\mathcal{M}))\cong\bigoplus_{w^{\prime\prime}\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I},w^{\prime\prime}<w}H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w^{\prime\prime}}(\mathcal{M})).

This together with the previous long exact sequence clearly finishes our induction step. The proof is thus finished.
∎

When
\pi^{\infty}
is
(I,I^{\prime})
-regular, we can define
H_{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
(for each
k\geq 0
) as the unique (up to isomorphism) admissible locally analytic representation such that

(4.11)

H_{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})^{\prime}\cong H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M}),

which exists by Proposition
4.7
and Corollary
4.9
. For each locally algebraic character
\chi
of
Z_{n,I}
, we write
H_{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})_{\chi}
for the
\chi
-isotypic component of
H_{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
, namely the unique (up to isomorphism) direct summand of
H_{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})
so that (
4.11
) induces an isomorphism

\left(H_{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})_{\chi}\right)^{\prime}\cong H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M})_{\chi^{\prime}}.

For each
w\in W(G_{n})
, we set
\chi_{w}^{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}|\cdot|\circ\delta_{w\cdot 0}
where
\delta_{w\cdot 0}:T_{n}\rightarrow E^{\times}
is the algebraic character associated with the weight
w\cdot 0\in X(T_{n})
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
I,I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be a pair of subsets and
\pi^{\infty}
an admissible smooth
E
-representation of
L_{n,I^{\prime}}
.
Assume that
\pi^{\infty}
is the subquotient of
i_{n,\emptyset,I^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\chi_{w}^{\infty})
for some
w\in W(G_{n})
, then
\pi^{\infty}
is
(I,I^{\prime})
-regular. In particular, the trivial representation
1_{L_{n,I^{\prime}}}
is
(I,I^{\prime})
-regular.

Proof.

According to Definition
4.8
, it is harmless to assume that
\pi^{\infty}=i_{n,\emptyset,I^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\chi_{w}^{\infty})
. According to the classical geometric lemma in representation theory (cf. Section VI.5.1 of
[
Ren
]
), we have

(4.12)

\left(i_{n,\emptyset}^{\infty}(\chi_{w}^{\infty})\right)_{N_{n,\emptyset}}\cong\bigoplus_{w^{\prime}\in W(G_{n})}\chi_{w^{\prime}}^{\infty}

and

(4.13)

\left(i_{n,\emptyset}^{\infty}(\chi_{w}^{\infty})\right)_{N_{n,I}}\cong\left(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\infty}i_{n,\emptyset,I^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\chi_{w}^{\infty})\right)_{N_{n,I}}\cong\bigoplus_{w^{\prime}\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{L_{n,I,w}}^{L_{n,I}}J_{n,I,I^{\prime},w}(\pi^{\infty}))^{\infty}.

Compare (
4.13
) with (
4.12
), we observe that the set
\Sigma_{w^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\pi^{\infty})
consists of elements of the form
\chi_{w^{\prime\prime}}^{\infty}|_{Z_{n,I}}
with
w^{\prime\prime}\in wW(L_{n,I^{\prime}})w^{\prime}W(L_{n,I})
. Given two elements
w^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime\prime\prime}\in W(G_{n})
, we have
\chi_{w^{\prime\prime}}^{\infty}|_{Z_{n,I}}=\chi_{w^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{\infty}|_{Z_{n,I}}
if and only if
w^{\prime\prime}W(L_{n,I})=w^{\prime\prime\prime}W(L_{n,I})
, and thus

\Sigma_{w^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\pi^{\infty})=\{\chi_{w^{\prime\prime}}^{\infty}|_{Z_{n,I}}\mid w^{\prime\prime}\in wW(L_{n,I^{\prime}})w^{\prime}W(L_{n,I})\}

are disjoint for different
w^{\prime}\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
. The last claim follows from the fact that
1_{L_{n,I^{\prime}}}
is a subrepresentation of
i_{n,\emptyset,I^{\prime}}^{\infty}(\chi_{1}^{\infty})
.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

Concerning the results up to Proposition
4.7
and Corollary
4.9
, it is actually possible to treat
\mathcal{M}
of the form
\mathcal{F}_{P_{n,I^{\prime}}}^{G_{n}}(V,\pi^{\infty})^{\prime}
, namely the dual of an arbitrary Orlik-Strauch representation, without assuming that
V
has the form
M^{\Delta_{n}}_{I^{\prime}}(\mu)
for some
\mu\in X(T_{n,E})
dominant for
L_{n,I^{\prime},E}\cap B_{n,E}
. In fact, each
V\in\mathcal{O}_{n,\Delta_{n}}^{I^{\prime}}
naturally determines a
D(G_{n})_{1}
-module
\mathcal{M}^{1}
which contains
V
as the (dense) subset of
\mathfrak{p}_{n,I^{\prime}}
-finite vectors and satisfies

\mathcal{M}\cong D(G_{n})\widehat{\otimes}_{D(G_{n})_{P_{n,I^{\prime}}}}(\mathcal{M}^{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}).

We can thus define
\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M})
,
\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M})
and
\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M})
by exactly the same formula as before. Note that the computation of such
H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathcal{M})
largely reduces to that of
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},V^{w})
for
V\in\mathcal{O}_{n,\Delta_{n}}^{I^{\prime}}
and
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, which is complicated in general.

Remark 4.12
.

Proposition
4.7
is actually a true generalization of the classical geometric lemma in smooth representation theory. According to Remark
4.11
, we can simply take
\mathcal{M}^{1}
to be the trivial
D(G_{n})_{1}
-module, in which case we have

\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M})=(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime},

\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M})=\widehat{D}^{\infty}(N_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D^{\infty}(N_{n,I,w})}(\pi^{\infty,w})^{\prime}\cong\left(\rm{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Ind}_{N_{n,I,w}}^{N_{n,I}}\pi^{\infty,w}\right)^{\prime}

and

\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M})\cong D^{\infty}(L_{n,I})\widehat{\otimes}_{D^{\infty}(L_{n,I,w})}\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M})\cong\left(\rm{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Ind}_{P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}}^{P_{n,I^{\prime}}^{w}P_{n,I}}\pi^{\infty,w}\right)^{\prime}.

For each
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, we write
\mathcal{O}_{n,I}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}_{\rm{alg}}^{\mathfrak{l}_{n,I,E}\cap\mathfrak{b}_{n,E}}
for the BGG category
\mathcal{O}
attached to
\mathfrak{l}_{n,I,E}
and
\mathfrak{l}_{n,I,E}\cap\mathfrak{b}_{n,E}
.
For each
\mu\in X(T_{n,E})
which is dominant for
L_{n,I,E}\cap B_{n,E}
, we write
L^{I}(\mu)
for the unique simple object in
\mathcal{O}_{n,I}
with highest weight
\mu
,
M^{\Delta_{n}}_{I}(\mu)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}U(\mathfrak{g}_{n,E})\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{p}_{n,I,E})}L^{I}(\mu)
for the generalized Verma module, and
\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}
for the maximal subcategory of
\mathcal{O}_{n,I}
so that
U(\mathfrak{z}_{n,I,E})
acts on each object of
\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}
by the same character as that of
M^{\Delta_{n}}_{I}(\mu)
. More generally, we write
\mathcal{O}_{n,I}^{I^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{n,I}
for the parabolic BGG category corresponding to
\mathfrak{l}_{n,I,E}\cap\mathfrak{p}_{n,I^{\prime},E}\supseteq\mathfrak{l}_{n,I,E}\cap\mathfrak{b}_{n,E}
and
\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}^{I^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\mathcal{O}_{n,I}^{I^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}
.
For a
U(\mathfrak{z}_{n,I,E})
-module
V
, we write
V_{n,I,\mu}
for its maximal quotient on which
U(\mathfrak{z}_{n,I,E})
acts by the same character as that of
M^{\Delta_{n}}_{I}(\mu)
, and in particular
V\mapsto V_{n,I,\mu}
gives a projection functor
\mathcal{O}_{n,I}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}
. We assume from now that
\mu=-\lambda
where
\lambda\in X(T_{n,E})
is dominant with respect to
B_{n,E}^{+}
.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
I,I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be two subsets. Then we have

(i)

H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},V)_{n,I,\mu}=0
for each
V\in\mathcal{O}_{n,\Delta_{n},\mu}
and each
k\geq 1
;

(ii)

H^{0}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},-)_{n,I,\mu}
induces an exact functor
\mathcal{O}_{n,\Delta_{n},\mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}
; and

(iii)

H^{0}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},M^{\Delta_{n}}_{I^{\prime}}(\mu))_{n,I,\mu}\cong F_{n,I}(\lambda)^{\prime}
for each
I\subseteq I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
.

Proof.

Let
V
be an object in
\mathcal{O}_{n,\Delta_{n},\mu}
. We can compute
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},V)
as the cohomology of the Chevalley–Eilenberg complex with the
k
-th term given by
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},V)
. Note that
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},V)
is semisimple as a
U(\mathfrak{z}_{n,I,E})
-module and contains
H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},V)
as a
U(\mathfrak{z}_{n,I,E})
-submodule. Then we conclude by the observation that the
U(\mathfrak{z}_{n,I,E})
-module
\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},V)\cong V\otimes_{E}\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E}^{+}
(with
\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E}^{+}
the Lie algebra of the unipotent radical of the parabolic subgroup opposite to
P_{n,I,E}\subseteq G_{n,E}
) satisfies
(V\otimes_{E}\wedge^{k}\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E}^{+})_{n,I,\mu}=0
for each
k\geq 1
. The vanishing of
H^{1}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},-)_{n,I,\mu}
implies the exactness of
H^{0}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},-)_{n,I,\mu}
. It is not difficult to see that, for each simple object
L^{\Delta_{n}}(\mu^{\prime})\in\mathcal{O}_{n,\Delta_{n},\mu}
, we have

H^{0}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},L^{\Delta_{n}}(\mu^{\prime}))\cong L^{I}(\mu^{\prime})

and thus

H^{0}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},L^{\Delta_{n}}(\mu^{\prime}))_{n,I,\mu}\neq 0

if and only if
\mu^{\prime}=w\cdot\mu
for some element
w\in W(L_{n,I,E})
(in the Weyl group of
L_{n,I,E}
). Now let
I\subseteq I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be two subsets, then the surjection
M^{\Delta_{n}}_{I^{\prime}}(\mu)\twoheadrightarrow L^{\Delta_{n}}(\mu)
induces a surjection

H^{0}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},M^{\Delta_{n}}_{I^{\prime}}(\mu))_{n,I,\mu}\twoheadrightarrow H^{0}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},L^{\Delta_{n}}(\mu))_{n,I,\mu}\cong L^{I}(\mu).

If this surjection is not an isomorphism, then there exists
1\neq w\in W(L_{n,I,E})
such that
w\cdot\mu
is dominant with respect to
B_{n,E}\cap L_{n,I^{\prime},E}
, but this is impossible as
I\subseteq I^{\prime}
.
∎

We assume from now on that
\pi^{\infty}=1_{L_{n,I^{\prime}}}
, which implies that
\mathrm{gr}_{w}^{1}(\mathcal{M})=(\mathcal{M}^{1})^{w}
for each
w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
. We continue to assume that
\mu=-\lambda
where
\lambda\in X(T_{n,E})
is dominant with respect to
B_{n,E}^{+}
.
Note that
Z_{n,I}
acts on
F_{n,I}(\lambda)
by an algebraic character
\delta_{n,I,\lambda}
.

Lemma 4.14
.

If
w=1
and thus
C_{1}=\overline{C_{1}}=Y_{1}=\overline{Y_{1}}=P_{n,I^{\prime}}P_{n,I}
is the closed cell, then we have

(i)

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathcal{M}))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}=0
if
k\geq 1
;

(ii)

H^{0}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathcal{M}))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}=F_{n,I}(\lambda)
if
I\subseteq I^{\prime}
; and

(iii)

H^{0}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathcal{M}))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}^{L_{n,I}}(V,1_{L_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}})
for some
V\in\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}^{I\cap I^{\prime}}
if
I\not\subseteq I^{\prime}
.

Proof.

When
w=1
, we have
N_{n,I}=N_{n,I,w}
,
L_{n,I,1}=L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I^{\prime}}
and
\mathrm{gr}_{1}^{\rm{u}}(\mathcal{M})=\mathrm{gr}_{1}^{1}(\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{M}^{1}
. The key observation is Proposition
4.7
induces an isomorphism

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathcal{M}))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{L_{n,I,1}}^{L_{n,I}}(H^{k}(\mathfrak{n}_{n,I,E},\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat})_{n,I,\mu},(\pi^{\infty})_{N_{n,I,1}})^{\prime}

with
N_{n,I,1}
being the unipotent radical of
L_{n,I,1}
. As
\mathcal{M}^{1,\flat}=M^{\Delta_{n}}_{I^{\prime}}(\mu)
, the desired results follow directly from Lemma
4.13
.
∎

Lemma 4.15
.

If
1\neq w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
, then we have

H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}=0

for all
k\geq 0
.

Proof.

This follows directly from Proposition
4.7
, Lemma
4.10
and Lemma
4.14
. In fact, Lemma
4.10
already implies that, if
H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{1}(\mathcal{M}))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}\neq 0
for certain
k\geq 0
, then
H^{k}(N_{n,I},\mathrm{gr}_{w}(\mathcal{M}))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}\neq 0
for all
k\geq 0
and
1\neq w\in W_{n}^{I^{\prime},I}
.
∎

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
I,I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be two subsets. Then we have

(i)

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\cong H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})
for each
k\geq 0
, if
I\subseteq I^{\prime}
; and

(ii)

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))=0
for each
k\geq 0
, if
I\not\subseteq I^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Recall that we write
\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{D(G_{n}),\lambda}
(resp.
\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{D(L_{n,I}),\lambda}
) for the
\mathrm{Ext}
-groups between abstract
D(G_{n})
-modules (resp.
D(L_{n,I})
-modules) fixing
Z_{n}
-character (which equals that of
F_{n,I}(\lambda)^{\prime}
). There exists a standard spectral sequence (see Lemma
1.8
)

\mathrm{Ext}^{k_{1}}_{D(L_{n,I}),\lambda}(F_{n,I}(\lambda)^{\prime},H^{k_{2}}(N_{n,I},i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)^{\prime}))\Rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}_{D(G_{n}),\lambda}(i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)^{\prime},i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)^{\prime})=\mathrm{Ext}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)).

It follows from Lemma
4.14
and Lemma
4.15
that

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{L_{n,I},\lambda}(H_{0}(N_{n,I},i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}},F_{n,I}(\lambda)).

If
I\subseteq I^{\prime}
, we have

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{L_{n,I},\lambda}(H_{0}(N_{n,I},i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}},F_{n,I}(\lambda))\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{L_{n,I},\lambda}(F_{n,I}(\lambda),F_{n,I}(\lambda))\cong H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})

where the second isomorphism follows from for example the translation functor in
[
JL21
]
.
If
I\not\subseteq I^{\prime}
, then it suffices to show that

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{L_{n,I},\lambda}(\mathcal{F}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}^{L_{n,I}}(V,1_{L_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}),F_{n,I}(\lambda))=0

for each
V\in\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}^{I\cap I^{\prime}}
and
k\geq 0
. As each
V\in\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}^{I\cap I^{\prime}}
admits BGG resolution by generalized Verma modules in
\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\mu}^{I\cap I^{\prime}}
, we reduce to the case when
V
has the form
M^{I}_{I\cap I^{\prime}}(\omega)=U(\mathfrak{l}_{n,I})\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{l}_{n,I}\cap\mathfrak{p}_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}})}F_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}(\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{n,I,\lambda}^{I\cap I^{\prime}}
where
\omega\in X(T_{n})
is a weight dominant for
\mathfrak{b}_{n}\cap\mathfrak{l}_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}
. Then it follows from Proposition 6.5 of
[
ST05
]
that

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{L_{n,I},\lambda}(\mathcal{F}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}^{L_{n,I}}(V,1_{L_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}),F_{n,I}(\lambda))\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{L_{n,I},\lambda}(i_{n,I,I\cap I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(F_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}(\omega)),F_{n,I}(\lambda))\\
\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{\ell}_{L_{n,I},-\lambda}(F_{n,I}(\lambda)^{\vee},i_{n,I,I\cap I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(F_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}(\omega)^{\vee}\otimes_{E}\mathfrak{d}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}))

for each
k,\ell\geq 0
satisfying
\ell=k+\mathrm{dim}L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}-\mathrm{dim}L_{n,I}
.
Here
\mathfrak{d}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}
is locally algebraic character of
L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}
with non-trivial smooth part as
I\not\subseteq I^{\prime}
. Hence,
F_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}(\omega)^{\vee}\otimes_{E}\mathfrak{d}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}
and
H_{\ell_{2}}(N_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}},F_{n,I}(\lambda)^{\vee})
never share the same
Z_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}
-character for any
\ell_{2}\geq 0
, which together with the spectral sequence

\mathrm{Ext}^{\ell_{1}}_{L_{n,I},-\lambda}(H_{\ell_{2}}(N_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}},F_{n,I}(\lambda)^{\vee}),F_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}(\omega)^{\vee}\otimes_{E}\mathfrak{d}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}})\\
\Rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}_{L_{n,I},-\lambda}(F_{n,I}(\lambda)^{\vee},i_{n,I,I\cap I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(F_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}(\omega)^{\vee}\otimes_{E}\mathfrak{d}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}))

implies that

\mathrm{Ext}^{\ell}_{L_{n,I},-\lambda}(F_{n,I}(\lambda)^{\vee},i_{n,I,I\cap I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(F_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}(\omega)^{\vee}\otimes_{E}\mathfrak{d}_{L_{n,I}\cap P_{n,I\cap I^{\prime}}}))=0

for each
\ell\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
I\subseteq I^{\prime}\subseteq I^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be three subsets and
k,k^{\prime}\geq 0
be two integers. Then the following commutative diagram is commutative.

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k^{\prime}}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\times\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\displaystyle{H^{k^{\prime}}(\overline{L}_{n,I^{\prime}},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I^{\prime}}})\times H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{H^{k^{\prime}}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})\times H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k+k^{\prime}}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\displaystyle{H^{k+k^{\prime}}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})}

Proof.

It is clear that
\mathrm{Hom}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))
is one dimensional and spanned by the natural embedding
\iota:i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
. Moreover, Proposition
4.16
also implies that the cup product map

\mathrm{Hom}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\times\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\xrightarrow{\cup}\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))

induces an isomorphism

\iota\cup\bullet:\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))

which corresponds to identity map of
H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})
. For each
x\in\mathrm{Ext}^{k^{\prime}}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))
and
y\in\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))
, there exists
y^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))
such that
y=\iota\cup y^{\prime}
, and thus
x\cup y=(x\cup\iota)\cup y^{\prime}
. We finish the proof by noting that the map

\bullet\cup\iota:\mathrm{Ext}^{k^{\prime}}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{k^{\prime}}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))

corresponds to the restriction map
H^{k^{\prime}}(\overline{L}_{n,I^{\prime}},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I^{\prime}}})\rightarrow H^{k^{\prime}}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})
under Proposition
4.16
.
∎

We use the shortened notation
H_{I}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}H^{\bullet}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})
,
H_{I^{\prime}}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}H^{\bullet}(\overline{L}_{n,I^{\prime}},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I^{\prime}}})
and write
\mathrm{Res}_{n,I,I^{\prime}}^{\bullet}:H_{I}^{\bullet}\rightarrow H_{I^{\prime}}^{\bullet}
for the restriction map, for each
I^{\prime}\subseteq I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
.
We consider two pair of subsets
I_{0}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
and
I_{2}\subseteq I_{3}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
satisfying
I_{3}\subseteq I_{1}
and
I_{1}\setminus I_{0}=I_{3}\setminus I_{2}=\{i\}
for some
i\in\Delta_{n}
. Let
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in E^{\times}
be two scalars. We define a complex
\mathbf{C}_{\theta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}[i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)]
(resp.
\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}[i_{n,I_{3}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow i_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)]
) supported in degree
[-1,0]
with the injection given by
\theta\kappa_{I_{1},I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
(resp.
\theta^{\prime}\kappa_{I_{3},I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
). We have canonical maps
p_{\theta}:i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{C}_{\theta}
and
q_{\theta}:\mathbf{C}_{\theta}\rightarrow i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]
and similarly for
p_{\theta^{\prime}}
and
q_{\theta^{\prime}}
. In the following, we will use the isomorphisms in item
(i)
Proposition
4.16
without further explanation. It follows directly from Proposition
4.16
that

•

\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I_{3}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))=0
;

•

\kappa_{I_{1},I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
induces an isomorphism
\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))
which corresponds to identity map on
H_{I_{2}}^{\bullet}
;

•

\kappa_{I_{3},I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
induces a map
\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I_{3}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))

which corresponds to
\mathrm{Res}_{n,I_{3},I_{2}}^{\bullet}:H_{I_{3}}^{\bullet}\rightarrow H_{I_{2}}^{\bullet}
.

Consequently, for each
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in E^{\times}
, we have

•

\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\theta},i_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))=0
as we can compute it using a spectral sequence whose second page is identically zero;

•

q_{\theta^{\prime}}
and
q_{\theta}
induce isomorphisms
\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\theta},\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{q_{\theta^{\prime},\ast}}\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\theta},i_{n,I_{3}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1])\xleftarrow{q_{\theta}^{\ast}}H_{I_{3}}^{\bullet}
;

•

p_{\theta^{\prime}}
induces a isomorphism
H_{I_{2}}^{\bullet}\xrightarrow{p_{\theta^{\prime},\ast}}\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})
.

The following is our main goal.

Lemma 4.18
.

The composition

H_{I_{3}}^{\bullet}\xrightarrow{(q_{\theta^{\prime},\ast})^{-1}q_{\theta}^{\ast}}\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\theta},\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{p_{\theta}^{\ast}}\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{p_{\theta^{\prime},\ast}^{-1}}H_{I_{2}}^{\bullet}

is given by
\theta^{\prime}\theta^{-1}\mathrm{Res}_{n,I_{3},I_{2}}^{\bullet}
.

Proof.

Let
k\geq 0
be an integer. It follows from item
(i)
of Lemma
1.7
that we can compute
\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})
via a spectral sequence whose degenerate at its second page due to degree reason. Therefore we have a canonical isomorphism in the derived category of
E
-vector spaces

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\cong H_{\theta^{\prime}}^{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}[H_{I_{3}}^{k}\rightarrow H_{I_{2}}^{k}]

with RHS supported in degree
[0,1]
and the map given by
\theta^{\prime}\mathrm{Res}_{n,I_{3},I_{2}}^{k}
. The distinguished triangle

(4.14)

i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)\xrightarrow{p_{\theta}}\mathbf{C}_{\theta}\xrightarrow{q_{\theta}}i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]\rightarrow

determines the distinguish triangle

(4.15)

\mathbf{C}_{\theta}\xrightarrow{q_{\theta}}i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]\xrightarrow{\theta\kappa_{I_{1},I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]}i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]\xrightarrow{p_{\theta}[-1]}.

This induces a distinguish triangle

(4.16)

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{\theta\kappa_{I_{1},I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]^{\ast}}\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{q_{\theta}^{\ast}}\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\theta},\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{p_{\theta}^{\ast}}.

The commutative diagram

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta\kappa_{I_{1},I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]^{\ast}}
\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})}
\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],i_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\theta\kappa_{I_{1},I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]^{\ast}}
\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],i_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\cong}

implies that
\theta\kappa_{I_{1},I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1]^{\ast}
in (
4.16
) corresponds to the composition
H_{I_{2}}^{k}[1]\xrightarrow{\theta}H_{I_{2}}^{k}[1]\xrightarrow{f_{\theta^{\prime}}}H_{\theta^{\prime}}^{k}
with the second map
f_{\theta^{\prime}}
being the standard truncation map. On the other hand, using the commutative diagram

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{\theta}^{\ast}}
\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\theta},\mathbf{C}_{\theta^{\prime}})}
\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-1],i_{n,I_{3}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{q_{\theta}^{\ast}}
\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\theta},i_{n,I_{3}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\cong}

we know that the map
q_{\theta}^{\ast}
in (
4.16
) corresponds to the truncation map
g_{\theta^{\prime}}:H_{\theta^{\prime}}^{k}\rightarrow H_{I_{3}}^{k}
.
Consequently, we deduce from (
4.16
) the following distinguish triangle

H_{I_{2}}^{k}[1]\xrightarrow{\theta f_{\theta^{\prime}}}H_{\theta^{\prime}}^{k}\xrightarrow{g_{\theta^{\prime}}}H_{I_{3}}^{k}\xrightarrow{(q_{\theta^{\prime},\ast})^{-1}q_{\theta}^{\ast}p_{\theta}^{\ast}p_{\theta^{\prime},\ast}^{-1}}.

If we compare it with the obvious distinguish triangle

H_{I_{2}}^{k}[1]\xrightarrow{f_{\theta^{\prime}}}H_{\theta^{\prime}}^{k}\xrightarrow{g_{\theta^{\prime}}}H_{I_{3}}^{k}\xrightarrow{\theta^{\prime}\mathrm{Res}_{n,I_{3},I_{2}}^{k}}

from the definition of
H_{\theta^{\prime}}^{k}
, we conclude that
(q_{\theta^{\prime},\ast})^{-1}q_{\theta}^{\ast}p_{\theta}^{\ast}p_{\theta^{\prime},\ast}^{-1}=\theta^{-1}\theta^{\prime}\mathrm{Res}_{n,I_{3},I_{2}}^{k}
, which finishes the proof.
∎

4.2.
Extensions between two complex

In this section, we compute the
\mathrm{Ext}
-groups between certain complexes (with each term given by direct sum of various
i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
) using the computation of
E_{\bullet,I_{0},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
in Section
2
and Proposition
4.16
.

We first introduce here some general complexes that will be essential in Section
4.3
. Note that
\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda)
(for various
I_{0},I_{1}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
) are special examples of the more general complexes we consider here.

We consider a tuple
\mathcal{P}=(\underline{I},\underline{\ell}^{+},\underline{\ell}^{-})
that satisfies the following conditions

•

\underline{I}=(I_{r^{\prime}})_{1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r}
with
\Delta_{n}=\bigsqcup_{r^{\prime}=1}^{r}I_{r^{\prime}}
a partition;

•

\underline{\ell}^{+}=(\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+})_{1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r}
and
\underline{\ell}^{-}=(\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{-})_{1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r}
with
0\leq\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{-}\leq\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}\leq\#I_{r^{\prime}}
for each
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
.

Given a tuple
\mathcal{P}
as above, we define a complex
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda)
whose degree
-\ell
term is the direct sum of all
i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
satisfying
\#I^{\prime}\cap I_{r^{\prime}}\in[\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{-},\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}]
for each
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
.

Given an extra
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, we can compute
\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))
by a spectral sequence
{}_{\mathcal{P}}E_{\bullet,I}^{\bullet,\bullet}
(see item
(ii)
of Lemma
1.7
) with
{}_{\mathcal{P}}E_{1,I}^{-\ell,k}
isomorphic to the direct sum of

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\cong H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})

for
I\subseteq I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
satisfying
\#I^{\prime}=\ell
and
\#I^{\prime}\cap I_{r^{\prime}}\in[\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{-},\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}]
for each
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
. For three integers
\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
, we use the shorted notation
c(\ell,\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\frac{(\ell-\ell^{\prime\prime})!}{(\ell-\ell^{\prime})!(\ell^{\prime}-\ell^{\prime\prime})!}
. We fix a
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
for now and set
\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\max\{\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{-},\#I\cap I_{r^{\prime}}\}
. We set
d_{r^{\prime}}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}c(\#I_{r^{\prime}},\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+},\#I\cap I_{r^{\prime}})
if
\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}=\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}
,
d_{r^{\prime}}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}0
if
\#I_{r^{\prime}}=\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}>\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}
, and
d_{r^{\prime}}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}c(\#I_{r^{\prime}},\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+},1+\#I\cap I_{r^{\prime}})
if
\#I_{r^{\prime}}>\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}>\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}
. We also set
d_{I,r^{\prime}}^{-}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}0
if
\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}>\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}=\#I\cap I_{r^{\prime}}
, and
d_{I,r^{\prime}}^{-}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}c(\#I_{r^{\prime}}-1,\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-},\#I\cap I_{r^{\prime}})
if
\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}>\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}>\#I\cap I_{r^{\prime}}
.

Then we consider the complex
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}}
of
E
-vector spaces (having support in degree
[\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-},\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}]
) with the dimension of the degree
\ell_{r^{\prime}}
term of
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}}
counting the number of sets
I^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
satisfying
I\cap I_{r^{\prime}}\subseteq I^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}\subseteq I_{r^{\prime}}
and
\#I^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}=\ell_{r^{\prime}}\in[\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-},\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}]
. The differential of
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}}
is naturally induced from inclusion between various
I^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
. We write
H^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}})
for its cohomology (understood to be a complex with zero differentials) and observe that
H^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}})=0
if
\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}>\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}
,
H^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}})=E^{\oplus d_{r^{\prime}}^{+}}[\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}]
if
\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}=\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}
, and
H^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}})=E^{\oplus d_{I,r^{\prime}}^{-}}[\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}]\oplus E^{\oplus d_{r^{\prime}}^{+}}[\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}]
if
\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}<\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}
.

Lemma 4.19
.

We have an isomorphism of complexes

(4.17)

_{\mathcal{P}}E_{1,I}^{\bullet,k}\cong H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})\otimes\bigotimes_{r^{\prime}=1}^{r}\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}}

which induces an isomorphism of cohomologies

{}_{\mathcal{P}}E_{2,I}^{\bullet,k}\cong H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})\otimes\bigotimes_{r^{\prime}=1}^{r}H^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}}),

and the spectral sequence
{}_{\mathcal{P}}E_{\bullet,I}^{\bullet,\bullet}
degenerates at the second page.
In particular, if
\ell_{r^{\prime},I}^{-}=\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}
for each
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
, then
{}_{\mathcal{P}}E_{2,I}^{\bullet,k}\cong H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})^{\oplus d_{\mathcal{P}}^{+}}[\ell_{\mathcal{P}}^{+}]
with
d_{\mathcal{P}}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{r^{\prime}=1}^{r}d_{r^{\prime}}^{+}
and
\ell_{\mathcal{P}}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\sum_{r^{\prime}=1}^{r}\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}
.

Proof.

We notice that the complex
{}_{\mathcal{P}}E_{1,I}^{\bullet,k}
is isomorphic to the tensor of
H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})
with a complex
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I}
of vector spaces, whose degree
\ell
term has dimension counting the number of
I\subseteq I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
satisfying
\#I^{\prime}=\ell
and
\#I^{\prime}\cap I_{r^{\prime}}\in[\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{-},\ell_{r^{\prime}}^{+}]
for each
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
. As each such
I^{\prime}
admits a partition
I^{\prime}=\bigsqcup_{r^{\prime}=1}^{r}I^{\prime}\cap I_{r^{\prime}}
, the complex
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I}
is isomorphic to the tensor product of complexes
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}}
for
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
with
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}}
defined before this lemma. We also note that
H^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I})
is clearly isomorphic to the tensor of various
H^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P},I,r^{\prime}})
. The decomposition (
4.17
) actually indicates that
{}_{\mathcal{P}}E_{\bullet,I}^{\bullet,\bullet}
is actually the tensor product of two spectral sequences, one supported inside the
0
-th row and the other supported inside the
0
-th column with both of them degenerate at the second page, which forces
{}_{\mathcal{P}}E_{\bullet,I}^{\bullet,\bullet}
to degenerate at the second page as well.
∎

The following is a special case of Lemma
4.19
when we take
r=3
and consider the partition
\Delta_{n}=I_{0}\sqcup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})\sqcup(\Delta_{n}\setminus I_{1})
with
\ell_{1}^{+}=\ell_{1}^{-}=\#I_{0}
,
\ell_{2}^{+}=\#I_{1}\setminus I_{0}
,
\ell_{2}^{-}=0
and
\ell_{3}^{+}=\ell_{3}^{-}=0
. Note that we have
\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda)\cong\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda)
in this case.

Lemma 4.20
.

For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\xleftarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ext}^{k-\#I_{1}}_{G_{n},\lambda}(i_{n,I_{1}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\cong H^{k-\#I_{1}}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})

if
I_{0}\cup I=I_{1}
and
\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))=0
otherwise.

Note that
I_{0}\cup I=I_{1}
if and only if
I_{1}\setminus I_{0}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{1}
. Now we consider a second pair of subsets
I_{2}\subseteq I_{3}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
. Using Lemma
4.20
, we can compute
\mathrm{Ext}^{\bullet}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))
by a spectral sequence
\{_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{r,I_{2},I_{3}}^{-\ell,k}\}_{r\geq 1,\#I_{2}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{3},k\geq 0}
whose first page has
(-\ell,k)
term given by

\bigoplus_{I_{2}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{3},\#I=\ell}\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\\
\cong\bigoplus_{I_{2}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{3},I_{0}\cup I=I_{1},\#I=\ell}H^{k-\#I_{1}}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})\cong\bigoplus_{I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})\subseteq I\subseteq I_{1}\cap I_{3},\#I=\ell}H^{k-\#I_{1}}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}}),

which is precisely
E_{1,I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0}),I_{1}\cap I_{3}}^{-\ell,k-\#I_{1}}
as defined at the beginning of Section
2.2
. The following cases are particularly simple.

Lemma 4.21
.

(i)

If
I_{2}\not\subseteq I_{1}
or
I_{1}\not\subseteq I_{0}\cup I_{3}
, then
\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))=0
for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(ii)

If
I_{0}\subseteq I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq I_{3}
, then
\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))\cong H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I_{1}},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I_{1}}})
for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. In particular,
\mathrm{Hom}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))
is spanned by the composition of two truncation morphisms
\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{1}}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda)
.

(iii)

The truncation map
\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{1}\cap I_{3}}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda)
induces an isomorphism of spectral sequences

{}_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{\bullet,I_{2},I_{1}\cap I_{3}}^{\bullet,\bullet}\xrightarrow{\sim}_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{\bullet,I_{2},I_{3}}^{\bullet,\bullet}.

Here we understand
\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{1}\cap I_{3}}(\lambda)
to be zero if
I_{2}\not\subseteq I_{1}
.

(iv)

If
I_{2}\subseteq I_{0}\subseteq I_{1}=I_{3}
, then we have a canonical isomorphism of spectral sequences

{}_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{\bullet,I_{2},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}\cong_{I_{0}\setminus I,I_{1}}E_{\bullet,I_{2}\setminus I,I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}

for each
I\subseteq I_{2}
.

Proof.

The first part follows from the observation that
I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})\subseteq I_{1}\cap I_{3}
is equivalent to
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq I_{0}\cup I_{3}
. For the second part, it suffices to note that
I_{0}\subseteq I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq I_{3}
implies that
I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})=I_{1}=I_{1}\cap I_{3}
, and thus

\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))\cong_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{1,I_{2},I_{3}}^{-\#I_{1},k+\#I_{1}}\cong E_{1,I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0}),I_{1}\cap I_{3}}^{-\#I_{1},k}\cong H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I_{1}},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I_{1}}}).

In particular,
\mathrm{Hom}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))
is one dimensional and thus spanned by
\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{1}}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda)
. The third part follows from the following isomorphism of first page of spectral sequences

{}_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{1,I_{2},I_{3}}^{-\ell,k}\cong E_{1,I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0}),I_{1}\cap I_{3}}^{-\ell,k-\#I_{1}}\cong_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{1,I_{2},I_{1}\cap I_{3}}^{-\ell,k}.

The fourth part follows from the isomorphism of first page of spectral sequences

{}_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{\bullet,I_{2},I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}\cong E_{1,I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0}),I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet-\#I_{1}}\cong_{I_{0}\setminus I,I_{1}}E_{1,I_{2}\setminus I,I_{1}}^{\bullet,\bullet}

based on the equality
I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})=(I_{2}\setminus I)\cup(I_{1}\setminus(I_{0}\setminus I))
.
∎

Now we apply computations from Section
2.2
to
{}_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{r,I_{2},I_{3}}^{-\ell,k}\cong E_{1,I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0}),I_{1}\cap I_{3}}^{-\ell,k-\#I_{1}}
. Lemma
2.27
implies that
\{_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{r,I_{2},I_{3}}^{-\ell,k}\}_{r\geq 1,\#I_{2}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{3},k\geq 0}
degenerates at the second page. Hence, we deduce from Lemma
2.16
and Proposition
2.26
that

Theorem 4.22
.

Let
I_{0}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
and
I_{2}\subseteq I_{3}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be two pairs of subsets with
I_{0}^{\sharp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})
and
I_{1}^{\sharp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}I_{1}\cap I_{3}
.

•

If
\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))\neq 0
, then we have
I_{2}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq I_{0}\cup I_{3}
and
\#I_{1}^{\sharp}-2\#I_{0}^{\sharp}+\#I_{1}\leq h\leq n^{2}-n+\#I_{1}
.

•

For each
h\in\{\#I_{1}^{\sharp}-2\#I_{0}^{\sharp}+\#I_{1},\#I_{1}^{\sharp}-2\#I_{0}^{\sharp}+\#I_{1}+1\}
, the space

M_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}^{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{\#I_{1}^{\sharp}-2\#I_{0}^{\sharp}+\#I_{1}}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))

admits a canonical decreasing filtration

0=\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{0}^{\sharp}+1}(M_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}^{h})\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{0}^{\sharp}}(M_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}^{h})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{1}^{\sharp}}(M_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}^{h})=M_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}^{h}

such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{-\ell}(M_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}^{h})/\mathrm{Fil}^{-\ell+1}(M_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}^{h})\cong E_{2,I_{0}^{\sharp},I_{1}^{\sharp}}^{-\ell,\ell+h-\#I_{1}}
admits a basis indexed by
\Psi_{I_{0}^{\sharp},I_{1}^{\sharp}}^{-\ell,\ell+h-\#I_{1}}
for each
\#I_{0}^{\sharp}\leq\ell\leq\#I_{1}^{\sharp}
.

Recall that
\mathbf{C}_{I,\Delta_{n}}(\lambda)\cong V_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)[-\#I]
for each
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
. If we take
I_{1}=I_{3}=\Delta_{n}
, we have
I_{0}^{\sharp}=I_{2}\cup(\Delta_{n}\setminus I_{0})\subseteq\Delta_{n}=I_{1}^{\sharp}
. We set

h_{I_{0},I_{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}(\#I_{1}^{\sharp}-2\#I_{0}^{\sharp}+\#I_{1})+\#I_{2}-\#I_{0}=2n-2-2\#I_{2}\cup(\Delta_{n}\setminus I_{0})+\#I_{2}-\#I_{0}\\
=2n-2+2\#I_{2}\cap(\Delta_{n}\setminus I_{0})-2\#I_{2}-2\#\Delta_{n}\setminus I_{0}+\#I_{2}-\#I_{0}=2\#I_{2}\setminus I_{0}+\#I_{0}-\#I_{2}\\
=\#I_{0}+\#I_{2}-2\#I_{0}\cap I_{2}=\#I_{0}\setminus I_{2}+\#I_{2}\setminus I_{0}.

We observe that
I_{2}\subseteq I_{0}
if and only if
h_{I_{0},I_{2}}=\#I_{0}-\#I_{2}
, and
I_{0}\subseteq I_{2}
if and only if
h_{I_{0},I_{2}}=\#I_{2}-\#I_{0}
.
As a consequence of Theorem
4.22
, we obtain

Corollary 4.23
.

Let
I_{0},I_{2}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
be two subsets.

•

If
\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h}(V_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),V_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\neq 0
, then
h_{I_{0},I_{2}}\leq h\leq n^{2}-1+\#I_{2}-\#I_{0}
.

•

The space
\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h_{I_{0},I_{2}}}(V_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),V_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))
admits a canonical decreasing filtration

0=\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{0}^{\sharp}+1}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}})\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{0}^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{-n+1}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}})=\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}

such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{-\ell}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}})/\mathrm{Fil}^{-\ell+1}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}})\cong E_{2,I_{0}^{\sharp},\Delta_{n}}^{-\ell,\ell+n-1-2\#I_{0}^{\sharp}}
admits a basis indexed by
\Psi_{I_{0}^{\sharp},\Delta_{n}}^{-\ell,\ell+n-1-2\#I_{0}^{\sharp}}
for each
\#I_{0}^{\sharp}\leq\ell\leq n-1
.

•

The space
\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h_{I_{0},I_{2}}+1}(V_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),V_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))
admits a canonical decreasing filtration

0=\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{0}^{\sharp}+1}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{-\#I_{0}^{\sharp}}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}^{\prime})\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathrm{Fil}^{-n+1}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}^{\prime})=\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}^{\prime}

such that
\mathrm{Fil}^{-\ell}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}^{\prime})/\mathrm{Fil}^{-\ell+1}(\mathbf{E}_{I_{0},I_{2}}^{\prime})\cong E_{2,I_{0}^{\sharp},\Delta_{n}}^{-\ell,\ell+n-2\#I_{0}^{\sharp}}
admits a basis indexed by
\Psi_{I_{0}^{\sharp},\Delta_{n}}^{-\ell,\ell+n-\#I_{0}^{\sharp}}
for each
\#I_{0}^{\sharp}\leq\ell\leq n-1
.

Remark 4.24
.

Recall that
i_{n,I}^{\infty}=i_{n,I,\Delta_{n}}^{\infty}(1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})
and
V_{n,I}^{\infty}=i_{n,I}^{\infty}/\sum_{I\subsetneq I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}}i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\infty}
for each
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}
. Then for each pair
I_{0}\subseteq I_{1}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
, we can define a complex
\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{\infty}
supported in degree
[-\#I_{1},-\#I_{0}]
whose degree
-\ell
term is
\bigoplus_{I_{0}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{1},\#I=\ell}i_{n,I}^{\infty}
. For each
\lambda\in X(T_{n,E})
which is dominant with respect to
B_{n,E}
, we have
i_{n,I}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda)=F_{n,\Delta_{n}}(\lambda)\otimes_{E}i_{n,I}^{\infty}
,
V_{n,I}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda)=F_{n,\Delta_{n}}(\lambda)\otimes_{E}V_{n,I}^{\infty}
and
\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}F_{n,\Delta_{n}}(\lambda)\otimes_{E}\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{\infty}
. It follows from Theorem 7.1 of
[
Koh11
]
, Lemma
4.14
and Lemma
4.15
that the natural embedding
i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
induces isomorphisms

H^{k}(N_{n,I},i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)^{\prime})_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}\cong H^{k}(N_{n,I},i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda)^{\prime})_{\delta_{n,I,\lambda}^{\prime}}

for each
k\geq 0
, which together with standard spectral sequences implies the isomorphism

\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{k}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{k}(i_{n,I^{\prime}}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda),i_{n,I}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))

for each
I,I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
and
k\geq 0
. Consequently, if
I_{2}\subseteq I_{2}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
is a second pair of subsets, then
\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{2}}(\lambda))
and
\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{\bullet}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{2}}(\lambda))
can be computed by exactly the same spectral sequence, and thus isomorphic as filtered spaces (equipped with the canonical filtrations coming from the same spectral sequence). As a special case, we conclude that the natural embedding
V_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow V_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda)
induces an isomorphism

\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h}(V_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda),V_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ext}_{G_{n},\lambda}^{h}(V_{n,I_{0}}^{\rm{alg}}(\lambda),V_{n,I_{2}}^{\rm{an}}(\lambda))

for each
I_{0},I_{2}\subseteq\Delta_{n}
and
h\geq 0
.

4.3.
Explicit cup product

In this section, we explicitly compute certain cup product maps (see (
4.18
)) using canonical filtration on each
\mathrm{Ext}
-group as well as the basis of each graded piece established in Section
2
and Section
4.2
. The key new ingredients are the commutative diagrams (
4.20
) and (
4.23
) which carefully record the effect on
\mathrm{Ext}
-groups under various truncations of complexes.

Recall from Theorem
4.22
that we use the shortened notation
M^{k}_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}
for
\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{I_{2},I_{3}}(\lambda))
, and we will use similar notation for other
\mathrm{Ext}
-groups (for example
M^{k}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
for
\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(\lambda))
and
M^{k}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
for
\mathrm{Ext}^{k}_{G_{n},\lambda}(\mathbf{C}_{I_{0},I_{1}}(\lambda),\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(\lambda))
). We also set
H^{k}_{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\bigoplus_{I\in S}H^{k}(\overline{L}_{n,I},1_{\overline{L}_{n,I}})
for each set
S
of subsets of
\Delta_{n}
and
k\geq 0
.
Given two integers
k_{0},k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and three tuples
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}
as introduced at the beginning of Section
4.2
, we have a canonical cup product map

M^{k_{0}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\otimes M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}}\xrightarrow{\cup}M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}}.

In particular, given two integers
k_{0},k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
and three pairs of subsets of
\Delta_{n}

I_{0}\subseteq I_{1}
,
I_{2}\subseteq I_{3}
and
I_{4}\subseteq I_{5}
, we have the following cup product map

(4.18)

M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}}\otimes M^{k_{1}}_{I_{2},I_{3},I_{4},I_{5}}\xrightarrow{\cup}M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{4},I_{5}}.

We assume from now the following simplifying condition which is already sufficient for our later application.

Condition 4.25
.

We have
I_{1}=I_{3}=I_{5}
and
I_{4}\subseteq I_{2}\subseteq I_{0}
.

Let
J\subseteq[0,\#I_{2}\setminus I_{4}]
and
J^{\prime}\subseteq[0,\#I_{0}\setminus I_{2}]
be two subintervals. Under Condition
4.25
, we consider a list of tuples
\mathcal{P}_{J,J^{\prime}}
with a common (ordered) partition of set

\Delta_{n}=I_{4}\sqcup(I_{2}\setminus I_{4})\sqcup(I_{0}\setminus I_{2})\sqcup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})\sqcup(\Delta_{n}\setminus I_{1})

that satisfies
[\ell_{J,J^{\prime},1}^{-},\ell_{J,J^{\prime},1}^{+}]=\{\#I_{4}\}
,
[\ell_{J,J^{\prime},2}^{-},\ell_{J,J^{\prime},2}^{+}]=J
,
[\ell_{J,J^{\prime},3}^{-},\ell_{J,J^{\prime},3}^{+}]=J^{\prime}
,
[\ell_{J,J^{\prime},4}^{-},\ell_{J,J^{\prime},4}^{+}]=\{\#I_{1}\setminus I_{0}\}
and
[\ell_{J,J^{\prime},5}^{-},\ell_{J,J^{\prime},5}^{+}]=\{0\}
. Given two intervals
J_{i}=[\ell_{i}^{-},\ell_{i}^{+}]
for
i=1,2
, we write
J_{1}\leq J_{2}
if
\ell_{1}^{-}\leq\ell_{2}^{-}
and
\ell_{1}^{+}\leq\ell_{2}^{+}
. Given
J_{1},J_{2}\subseteq[0,\#I_{2}\setminus I_{4}]
and
J_{1}^{\prime},J_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq[0,\#I_{0}\setminus I_{2}]
satisfying
J_{1}\leq J_{2}
and
J_{1}^{\prime}\leq J_{2}^{\prime}
, then there exists a canonical morphism

(4.19)

\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P}_{J_{1},J_{1}^{\prime}}}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathbf{C}_{\mathcal{P}_{J_{2},J_{2}^{\prime}}}(\lambda).

We write
t_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\#I_{0}\setminus I_{2}
,
t_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\#I_{2}\setminus I_{4}
and let
s_{0}\in[0,t_{0}]
and
s_{1}\in[0,t_{1}]
be two integers. We define the following three set of subsets of
\Delta_{n}
.

•

S_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\{I\subseteq\Delta_{n}\mid I_{2}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})\subseteq I\subseteq I_{1},~\#I\cap(I_{0}\setminus I_{2})=s_{0}\}
.

•

S_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\{I\subseteq\Delta_{n}\mid I_{4}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{2})\subseteq I\subseteq I_{1},~\#I\cap(I_{2}\setminus I_{4})=s_{1}\}
.

•

S_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\{I\subseteq\Delta_{n}\mid I_{4}\cup(I_{1}\setminus I_{0})\subseteq I\subseteq I_{1},~\#I\cap(I_{2}\setminus I_{4})=s_{1},~\#I\cap(I_{0}\setminus I_{2})=s_{0}\}
.

For each
I\in S_{0}
(resp.
I\in S_{1}
, resp.
I\in S_{2}
), we have
\#I=\ell_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\#I_{2}+\#I_{1}\setminus I_{0}+s_{0}
(resp.
\#I=\ell_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\#I_{4}+\#I_{1}\setminus I_{2}+s_{1}
, resp.
\#I=\ell_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{\tiny{def}}}}{{=}}\#I_{4}+\#I_{1}\setminus I_{0}+s_{0}+s_{1}
). Note that we have
\ell_{0}+\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}=\#I_{2}+\#I_{1}\setminus I_{2}=\#I_{1}
and the map
S_{0}\times S_{1}\rightarrow S_{2}:~(I,I^{\prime})\mapsto I\cap I^{\prime}
is a bijection.

Assuming Condition
4.25
and using maps of the form (
4.19
), we can extend (
4.18
) to the following commutative diagram

(4.20)

\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\otimes}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{1}}_{I_{2},I_{1},I_{4},I_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{4},I_{1}}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\otimes}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{1}}_{I_{2},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{[0,s_{1}],[0,t_{0}]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\scriptstyle{p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{[0,s_{1}],[0,t_{0}]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{3}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\otimes}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{1}}_{I_{2},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[0,t_{0}]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\scriptstyle{p_{4}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[0,t_{0}]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{5}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},I_{2},I_{1}}}
\displaystyle{\otimes}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{1}}_{I_{2},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[s_{0},t_{0}]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\scriptstyle{p_{7}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[s_{0},t_{0}]}}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{6}}
\scriptstyle{p_{9}}
\displaystyle{\otimes}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{8}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}}
\displaystyle{\otimes}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\scriptstyle{p_{10}}
\displaystyle{M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}}
\displaystyle{H^{k_{0}+\ell_{0}-\#I_{1}}_{S_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{p_{11}}
\displaystyle{\otimes}
\displaystyle{H^{k_{1}+\ell_{1}-\#I_{1}}_{S_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\cong}
\displaystyle{H^{k_{0}+k_{1}+\ell_{2}-\#I_{1}}_{S_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\displaystyle{\cong}
\displaystyle{H^{k_{0}+\ell_{0}-\#I_{1}}_{S_{2}}}
\displaystyle{\otimes}
\displaystyle{H^{k_{1}+\ell_{1}-\#I_{1}}_{S_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\displaystyle{H^{k_{0}+k_{1}+\ell_{2}-\#I_{1}}_{S_{2}}}

The commutativity of (
4.20
) (including the definition of each map inside) is clear except that between the fourth and the fifth rows. In fact, we have an isomorphism between spectral sequences
{}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]}}E_{\bullet,\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}^{\bullet,\bullet}\xrightarrow{\sim}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]}}E_{\bullet,\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[s_{0},t_{0}]}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
from the isomorphism on their first pages. Consequently, there exists a canonical isomorphism
M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[s_{0},t_{0}]}}
and we can define
p_{7}

as the composition

(4.21)

M^{k_{1}}_{I_{2},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[s_{0},t_{0}]}}\rightarrow M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[s_{0},t_{0}]}}\cong M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}.

The commutativity between the fourth and the fifth row of (
4.20
) thus follows from the fact that the composition

M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}\otimes M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}M^{k_{0}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}\otimes M^{k_{1}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},[0,s_{0}]},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[s_{0},t_{0}]}}\xrightarrow{\cup}M^{k_{0}+k_{1}}_{I_{0},I_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\{s_{1}\},[s_{0},t_{0}]}}

clearly factors through
p_{8}
.
The isomorphism between the sixth and seventh rows is clear from the description of
{}_{I_{0},I_{1}}E_{1,\mathcal{P}_{\{t_{1}\},\{s_{0}\}}}^{\bullet,\bullet}
(resp.
